## Riya’s Foundling / 里娅的养子

===Original===
The loft of the feed-house, with its stacked grainsacks, was a B-72, a fort, a foxhole﻿—any number of things, depending on Phildee’s moods.
Today it was a jumping-off place.
Phildee slipped out of his dormitory and ran across the yard to the feed-house. He dropped the big wooden latch behind him, and climbed up the ladder to the loft, depending on the slight strength of his young arms more than on his legs, which had to be lifted to straining heights before they could negotiate the man-sized rungs.

===Chinese===
饲料房的阁楼，连同那一垛垛粮袋，可以是一架 B-72，一座堡垒，一个狐狸洞——可以是任何东西，全看菲尔迪（Phildee）当时的心情。
今天，它是一处出发之地。
菲尔迪溜出宿舍，跑过院子，奔进饲料房。他随手把大木闩闩上，顺着梯子往阁楼爬——与其说靠腿，不如说全凭两条小胳膊那点微薄的力气；那两条腿非得被吊到吃力的高处，才够得着那一级级供成人用的梯级。

===Original===
He reached the loft and stood panting, looking out over the farm through the loft door, at the light wooden fences around it, and the circling antenna of the radar tower.
Usually, he spent at least a little time each day crouched behind the grainsacks and being bigger and older, firing cooly and accurately into charging companies of burly, thick-lipped U.E.S. soldiers, or going over on one wing and whistling down on a flight of TT-34’s that scattered like frightened ducks before the fiery sleet of his wing rockets.
But today was different, today there was something he wanted to try.

===Chinese===
他上了阁楼，站在那儿喘气，透过阁楼门望向整个农场，望向农场四周轻巧的木栅栏，还有雷达塔上旋转环扫的天线。
平日里，他每天总要蹲在粮袋后面待上一小会儿，让自己变得更高大、更年长，朝着一队队猛冲过来的 U.E.S. 士兵——那些膀阔腰圆、嘴唇厚实的家伙——冷静而精准地开火；或是压下一侧机翼，呼啸着朝一队 TT-34 俯冲下去，看它们在他翼上火箭泼出的炽烈弹雨里，像受惊的鸭子一样四散奔逃。
可今天不一样，今天有件事他想试试。

===Original===
He stood up on his toes and searched. He felt the touch of Miss Cowan’s mind, no different from that of anyone else﻿—flat, unsystematic.
He sighed. Perhaps, somewhere, there was someone else like himself. For a moment, the fright of loneliness invaded him, but then faded. He took a last look at the farm, then moved away from the open door, letting his mind slip into another way of thinking.
His chubby features twisted into a scowl of concentration as he visualized reality. The scowl became a deeper grimace as he negated that reality, step by step, and substituted another.

===Chinese===
他踮起脚尖，开始搜寻。他触到了考恩小姐（Miss Cowan）的心智，跟谁的都没什么两样——平平坦坦，杂乱无章。
他叹了口气。也许在什么地方，还有一个像他一样的人。刹那间，孤独的恐惧侵入他的心，随即又淡去了。他最后望了一眼农场，便从敞开的门口挪开，任由心智滑进另一种思维方式。
他那圆嘟嘟的小脸拧成一副专注的皱眉，在心里把这个现实具象出来。当他一步一步否定这个现实、换上另一个时，那皱眉又加深成了一副怪相。

===Original===
> F is for Phildee.
> O is for Out.
> R is for Reimann.
> T is for Topology.
> H is for heartsick hunger.

===Chinese===
> F 者，菲尔迪也。
> O 者，出去也。
> R 者，黎曼（Reimann）也。
> T 者，拓扑也。
> H 者，蚀心的饥渴也。

===Original===
Abruptly, the Reimann fold became a concrete visualization. As though printed clearly in and around the air, which was simultaneously both around him and not around him, which existed/not existed in spacetime, he saw the sideslip diagram.
He twisted.
---

===Chinese===
倏然之间，黎曼折叠（Reimann fold）变成了具体的形象。那空气既在他四周、又不在他四周，在时空中存在而又不存在，图式仿佛清清楚楚地印在空气之中、空气之外——他看见了那幅侧滑图。
他一拧。
---

===Original===
Spring had come to Riya’s world; spring and the thousand sounds of it. The melted snow in the mountaintops ran down in traceries of leaping water, and the spring-crests raced along the creeks into the rivers. The riverbank grasses sprang into life; the plains turned green again.
Riya made her way up the path across the foothills, conscious of her shame. The green plain below her was dotted, two by two, with the figures of her people. It was spring, and Time. Only she was alone.
There was a special significance in the fact that she was here on this path in this season. The plains on either side of the brown river were her people’s territory. During the summer, the couples ranged over the grass until the dams were ready to drop their calves. Then it became the bulls’ duty to forage for their entire families until the youngsters were able to travel south to the winter range.

===Chinese===
春天来到了里娅（Riya）的世界；春天，还有春天的千百种声响。山顶融化的雪水化作缕缕跳跃的水流泻下，春汛沿着条条小溪奔进江河。河岸的草猛然恢复了生机；平原又绿了。
里娅沿着横穿山麓丘陵的小径向上走，心里清楚自己的羞耻。她脚下那片绿色平原上，一对一对地点缀着她的族人——「人族」（the people）——的身影。是春天了，也是时辰了。只有她孤身一个。
她在这个季节走上这条小径，这件事自有一层特别的含义。棕色大河两岸的平原是人族的领地。整个夏天，成双的伴侣在草地上游荡，直到母兽们快要产下幼崽。此后，为全家觅食便成了公兽的职责，直到小家伙们能够南迁，前往冬牧地。

===Original===
Through the space of years, the people had increased in numbers, the pressure of this steady growth making itself felt as the yearlings filled out on the winter range. It had become usual, as the slow drift northward was made toward the end of winter, for some of the people to split away from the main body and range beyond the gray mountains that marked the western limits of the old territories. Since these wanderers were usually the most willful and headstrong, they were regarded as quasi-outcasts by the more settled people of the old range.
But﻿—and here Riya felt the shame pierce more strongly than ever﻿—they had their uses, occasionally. Preoccupied in her shame, she involuntarily turned her head downward, anxious that none of the people be staring derisively upward at the shaggy brown hump of fur that was she, toiling up the path.
She was not the first﻿—but that was meaningless. That other female people had been ugly or old, that the same unforgotten force that urged her up the mountain path had brought others here before her, meant only that she was incapable of accepting the verdict of the years that had thinned her pelt, dimmed her eyes, and broken the smooth rhythm of her gait.

===Chinese===
岁月悠悠，人族的数目日渐增多；当满周岁的幼兽在冬牧地上渐渐长足膘时，这种稳步增长的压力便显露出来。每到冬末缓缓北移之时，总有些人族脱离大群，跑到灰山之外去游荡——那道灰山标出老领地的西界。这些流浪者多半最为任性倔强，因此被老牧地上更安分的族人看成半是弃民。
可是——在这里，里娅感到羞耻刺得比以往任何时候都更深——他们偶尔也有他们的用处。她一心沉在羞耻里，不由自主地把头低了下去，生怕有哪个族人正讥诮地朝上盯着她，盯着她这一团蓬乱的棕色毛丘，正吃力地往小径上挪。
她不是第一个——可这毫无意义。别的母族人从前也丑过、老过；那股同样难以忘怀的力量，也曾在她之前把她们引到这儿来。这一切只说明，她无法接受岁月作出的判决——那判决稀疏了她的皮毛，昏花了她的双眼，毁了她步态里原本流畅的节律。

===Original===
In short, it meant that Riya Sair, granddam times over, spurned by every male on the old range, was willing to cross the gray mountains and risk death from the resentful wild dams for the thin hope that there was a male among the wildlings who would sire her calf.
She turned her head back to the path and hurried on, cringing in inward self-reproach at her speed.
Except for her age, Riya presented a perfect average of her people. She stood two yards high and two wide at the shoulders, a yard at the haunches, and measured three and a half yards from her muzzle to the rudimentary tail. Her legs were short and stumpy, cloven-hooved. Her massive head hung slightly lower than her shoulders, and could be lowered to within an inch or two of the ground. She was herbivorous, ruminant, and mammalian. Moreover, she had intelligence﻿—not of a very high order, but adequate for her needs.

===Chinese===
简而言之，这意味着：里娅·赛尔（Riya Sair），做过好几重祖母的老母兽，被老牧地上的每一头公兽嫌弃，却甘愿翻越灰山，甘愿冒着被满怀怨怼的野群母兽踏死的风险，只为一线微弱的希望——但愿野群里有一头公兽，肯给她的幼崽当父亲。
她把头转回小径，加紧赶路，心里却又为自己的脚程暗暗自责。
若不是上了年纪，里娅就是人族再标准不过的平均样本。她站起身来肩高两码、肩宽两码，臀宽一码，从口鼻到那截退化的尾巴全长三码半。她的腿又短又粗，是偶蹄。那颗沉重的大头垂得比肩膀略低，还能一直低到离地只有一两英寸的地方。她食草，反刍，是哺乳动物。此外，她还有智能——算不上多高级，但应付自己的需要绰绰有余。

===Original===
From a Terrestrial point of view, none of this was remarkable. Many years of evolution had gone into her fashioning﻿—more years for her one species than for all the varieties of man that have ever been. Nevertheless, she did have some remarkable attributes.
It was one of these attributes that now enabled her to sense what happened on the path ahead of her. She stopped still, only her long fur moving in the breeze.
---

===Chinese===
从地球人的视点看，这一切毫不惊人。漫长的进化岁月铸成了她这副身量——单单她这一个物种所费的年头，就比人类有史以来所有变种加起来的还多。尽管如此，她确实有几种惊人的天赋。
此刻，正是其中一种天赋，让她察觉到前方小径上发生的事。她站住不动，只有一身长毛在微风里拂动。
---

===Original===
Phildee﻿—five, towheaded, round faced, chubby, dressed in a slightly grubby corduroy oversuit, and precocious﻿—had his attributes, too. Grubby and tousled; branded with a thread of licorice from one corner of his mouth to his chin; involved in the loss of his first milk-tooth, as he was﻿—he nevertheless slipped onto the path on Riya’s world, the highest product of Terrestrial evolution. Alice followed a white rabbit down a hole. Phildee followed Reimann down into a hole that, at the same time, followed him, and emerged﻿—where?
Phildee didn’t know. He could have performed the calculation necessary to the task almost instantly, but he was five. It was too much trouble.
He looked up, and saw a gray slope of rock vaulting above him. He looked down, and saw it fall away toward a plain on which were scattered pairs of foraging animals. He felt a warm breeze, smelled it, saw it blow dust along the path, and saw Riya:

===Chinese===
菲尔迪——五岁，淡黄头发，圆脸，胖乎乎，穿一身微微发脏的灯芯绒罩衣，又早慧——也有他的天赋。脏兮兮、乱蓬蓬；嘴角到下巴印着一道甘草糖渍；正赶上掉第一颗乳牙，就是这么一副样子——可他终究一步滑上了里娅世界的小径，成了地球进化的最高产物。爱丽丝（Alice）跟着一只白兔掉进兔子洞。菲尔迪跟着黎曼掉进一个洞里，那洞同时又跟着他往下掉，然后钻出来——到了哪儿？
菲尔迪不知道。他本可以几乎眨眼间就完成这项任务所需的计算，可他才五岁。太麻烦了。
他抬头，看见一道灰色岩坡在他头顶拱起。他低头，看见山坡倾落向一片平原，平原上散布着一对对觅食的兽。他感到一阵暖风，闻到它，看见它把尘土沿着小径吹动，还看见了里娅：

===Original===
> B is for big brown beast.
> L is for looming large, looking lonely.
> B? L? Bull? No﻿—bison.
> Bison: *bison (bi’sn)* n. *The buffalo of the N. Amer. plains.*

===Chinese===
> B 者，棕色大兽也。
> L 者，庞然耸大、望之孤单也。
> B？L？公牛？不——是野牛。
> 野牛：*bison (bi’sn)* 名词 *北美大平原上的野牛。*

===Original===
Phildee shook his head and scowled. No﻿—not bison, either. What, then? He probed.
Riya took a step forward. The sight of a living organism other than a person was completely unfamiliar to her. Nevertheless, anything that small, and undeniably covered﻿—in most areas, at least﻿—with some kind of fur, could not, logically, be anything but a strange kind of calf. But﻿—she stopped, and raised her head﻿—if a calf, then where was the call?
Phildee’s probe swept past the laboring mind directly into her telepathic, instinctual centers.
Voiceless, with their environment so favorable that it had never been necessary for them to develop prehensile limbs, female people had nevertheless evolved a method of child care commensurate with their comparatively higher intelligence.
Soft as tender fingers, gentle as the human hand that smooths the awry hair back from the young forehead, Riya’s mental caress enfolded Phildee.

===Chinese===
菲尔迪摇了摇头，皱起眉。不——也不是野牛。那是什么呢？他探了过去。
里娅向前迈了一步。看到一个活的生命而并非族中一员，这在她是完全陌生的事。不过，那么小的东西，又明明白白长着——至少大部分地方长着——某种皮毛，按道理讲，只能是一种奇特的幼崽。可是——她停下脚步，抬起头——若是幼崽，那呼唤在哪儿？
菲尔迪的探针掠过那颗吃力运转的心智，径直扎进她的传心本能中枢。
人族的母兽们没有嗓音，所处的环境又如此有利，以致从没必要进化出能抓握的肢体，可她们依然发展出了一套与她们相对更高的智能相称的育儿之法。
轻柔如温软的手指，温柔如抚平孩子额前乱发的那只人手，里娅的心灵爱抚把菲尔迪整个儿拢住了。

===Original===
Phildee recoiled. The feeling was:
Warm
Soft
Sweet
} *Not* candy in the mouth
Candy in the mouth {
Familiar
Good
Tasty
Nice
The feeling was {
Not Familiar
Not Good
Not Tasty
Not Nice

===Chinese===
菲尔迪猛地缩了回去。这种感觉是：
温暖
柔软
甜蜜
} *不是*嘴里的糖
嘴里的糖 {
熟悉
美好
可口
宜人
这种感觉是 {
不熟悉
不美好
不可口
不宜人

===Original===
> **Why?**:
> M is for many motionless months.
> T is for tense temper tantrums.
> R is for rabid﻿—*No!*﻿—rapid rolling wrench.
> M.T.R. Mother.

===Chinese===
> **为何？**：
> M 者，许多纹丝不动的月份也。
> T 者，一阵阵紧绷的脾气发作也。
> R 者，狂暴——*不！*——急速翻滚的扭转也。
> M.T.R.，母亲。

===Original===
Phildee’s mother wanted Phildee’s father. Phildee’s mother wanted green grass and apple trees, tight skirts and fur jackets on Fifth Avenue, men to turn and look, a little room where nobody could see her. Phildee’s mother had radiation burns. Phildee’s mother was dead.
He wavered; physically. Maintaining his position in this world was a process that demanded constant attention from the segment of his mind devoted to it. For a moment, even that small group of brain cells almost became involved in his reaction.
It was that which snapped him back into functioning logically. M.T.R. was Mother. Mother was:
Tall
Thin
White
Biped
} “In Heaven’s name, Doctor, when will this thing be over?”
B.L. was Riya. Riya was:

===Chinese===
菲尔迪的母亲想要菲尔迪的父亲。菲尔迪的母亲想要绿草地和苹果树，想要第五大道（Fifth Avenue）上的窄裙和皮草，想要男人回过头来看，想要一间谁也瞧不见她的小屋。菲尔迪的母亲有辐射烧伤。菲尔迪的母亲死了。
他晃了一晃；是身子在晃。要在这个世界里维持住自己的位置，需要他心智中专司此职的那一部分时刻不停地照看。有那么一瞬，连那一小簇脑细胞也差点卷进了他的反应。
正是这个，啪地把他弹回了逻辑运转。M.T.R. 是母亲。母亲是：
高
瘦
白
双足
}「看在老天的份上，大夫，这事情什么时候才是个完？」
B.L. 是里娅。里娅是：

===Original===
> Big brown beast, looming large, looking lonely.
> B.L.=M.T.R.
> Equation not meaningful, not valid.

---

===Chinese===
> 棕色大兽，庞然耸大，望之孤单。
> B.L.=M.T.R.
> 此等式无意义，不成立。

---

===Original===
Almost resolved, only a few traces of the initial conflict remained. Phildee put the tips of his right fingers to his mouth. He dug his toe into the ground, gouged a semicircular furrow, and smoothed it over with his sole.
Riya continued to look at him from where she was standing, two or three feet away. Haltingly, she reached out her mind again﻿—hesitating not because of fear of another such reaction on Phildee’s part, for that had been far beyond her capacity to understand, but because even the slightest rebuff on the part of a child to a gesture as instinctive as a Terrestrial mother’s caress was something that none of the people had ever encountered before.
While her left-behind intellectual capacity still struggled to reconcile the feel of childhood with a visual image of complete unfamiliarity, the warm mind-caress went gently forth again.

===Chinese===
几乎化解了，最初的冲突只剩下几丝残迹。菲尔迪把右手指尖抵在嘴上。他把脚趾踩进地面，抠出一道半圆的沟，再用脚掌把它抹平。
里娅站在两三英尺开外，仍旧望着他。她迟疑着又一次把心意伸了过去——迟疑不是怕菲尔迪再来一次那样的反应，那远远超出了她的理解力；而是因为，一个孩子对这样一个如同地球母亲的爱抚般出于本能的姿态，竟回以哪怕最轻微的拒绝——这样的事，人族之中谁也不曾遇见过。
她那落在后头的智能还在吃力地想把幼童的触感和全然陌生的视觉形象调和到一起，那道温暖的心灵爱抚又轻轻地送了出去。

===Original===
Phildee made up his mind. Ordinarily, he was immune to the small emotional problems that beclouded less rational intellects. He was unused to functioning in other than a cause/effect universe. Mothers were usually﻿—though sometimes not﻿—matronly women who spent the greater part of roughly twenty years per child in conscious preoccupation with, and/or subconscious or conscious rejection of, their offspring.
In his special case, Mother was a warm place, a frantic, hysteric voice, the pressure of the spasmodically contractile musculature linked to her hyperthyroid metabolism. Mother was a thing from before birth.
Riya﻿—Riya bore a strong resemblance to an intelligent cow. In any physiological sense, she could no more be his mother than﻿—

===Chinese===
菲尔迪拿定了主意。平常，那些把较欠理性的心智搅得昏昏然的小小情绪问题，根本奈何不了他。他不习惯在因果宇宙之外运作。母亲们通常是——虽说有时也不是——妇人模样的女人，每个孩子要占去大约二十年里的大半光阴，有意地牵肠挂肚，和/或下意识或有意识地排斥自己的骨肉。
在他这个特例里，母亲是一个温暖的地方，一个狂乱、歇斯底里的嗓音，一阵阵痉挛收缩的肌肉的压力，连着她那甲状腺机能亢进的新陈代谢。母亲是出生之前就在了的东西。
里娅——里娅活脱脱像一头有智能的母牛。从任何生理意义上说，她都不可能是他的母亲，正如——

===Original===
The second caress found him not unaccustomed to it. It enfolded his consciousness, tenderly, protectingly, empathetic.
Phildee gave way to instinct.
---

===Chinese===
第二次爱抚落下时，他已不觉得怎么生疏了。它拢住他的意识，温柔地，护持地，感同身受地。
菲尔迪听凭本能做主了。
---

===Original===
The fur along the ridge of Riya’s spine prickled with a well-remembered happiness as she felt the hesitant answering surge in Phildee’s mind. Moving surely forward, she nuzzled his face. Phildee grinned. He ran his fingers through the thick fur at the base of her short neck.
Big warm wall of brown fur.
Cool, happy nose.
Happy, happy, eyes.
Great joy welled up in Riya. No shameful trot across the mountains faced her now. No hesitant approach to the huddled, suspicious wildlings was before her. The danger of sharp female hooves to be avoided, of skulking at the edge of the herd in hope of an anxious male, was a thing no longer to be half-fearfully approached.

===Chinese===
当她感到菲尔迪心中泛起迟疑的回应时，里娅脊背隆线上的皮毛因一种记忆犹新的幸福而根根微微竖起。她笃定地凑上前去，用口鼻蹭他的脸。菲尔迪咧开嘴笑了。他把手指插进她短颈根部的厚毛里。
棕色皮毛砌成的温暖大墙。
清凉、快乐的鼻子。
快乐的、快乐的眼睛。
巨大的喜悦在里娅心中涌起。如今，再没有那趟丢脸的翻山小跑在前头等着她。再不用迟疑地凑近那些挤作一团、满腹狐疑的野群了。躲开母兽们锋利的蹄子、缩在兽群边上巴望一头急切公兽的那些风险，再也不必半带惊恐地去沾惹了。

===Original===
With a nudge of her head, she directed Phildee down the path to the old range while she herself turned around. She stood motionless for a sweeping scan of the plain below her. The couples were scattered over the grass﻿—but couples only, the females as yet unfulfilled.
This, too, was another joy to add to the greatest of all. So many things about her calf were incomprehensible﻿—the only dimly-felt overtones of projected symbology that accompanied Phildee’s emotional reactions, the alien structure﻿—so many, many things. Her mind floundered vainly through the complex data.
But all that was nothing. What did it matter? The Time had been, and for another season, she was a dam.
---

===Chinese===
她用头轻轻一拱，把菲尔迪引上通往老牧地的小径，自己则转过身来。她一动不动地站了片刻，把脚下的平原扫视了一圈。一对对伴侣散布在草地上——但只是成双而已，母兽们的心愿还没了结。
这又是添在至福之上的又一重欢喜。她的幼崽有那么多叫人琢磨不透的地方——伴随着菲尔迪情绪反应投射出的象征，她只能隐约觉出几分弦外之音，还有那全然陌生的构造——太多太多了。她的心智在纷繁的讯息里徒劳地扑腾。
可那都算不得什么。有什么要紧呢？时辰已经来过，这一季，她又是一头有崽的母兽了。
---

===Original===
Phildee walked beside her down the path, one fist wrapped in the fur of her flank, short legs windmilling.
They reached the plain, and Riya struck out across it toward the greatest concentration of people, her head proudly raised. She stopped once, and deliberately cropped a mouthful of grass with unconcern, but resumed her pace immediately thereafter.
With the same unconcern, she nudged Phildee into the center of the group of people, and, ignoring them, began teaching her calf to feed.

===Chinese===
菲尔迪走在她身旁下了小径，一只拳头攥着她腰侧的毛，两条短腿抡得像风车。
他们到了平原，里娅径直朝人族最密集的地方走去，头颅傲然扬起。她停了一回，故意满不在乎地啃了一口草，随后立刻又恢复了步子。
带着同一样的满不在乎，她把菲尔迪拱进人群中央，全不理会众族，开始教她的幼崽进食。

===Original===
Eat. (Picture of Phildee/calf on all fours, cropping the plains grass.)
Phildee stared at her in puzzlement. Grass was not food. He sent the data emphatically.
Riya felt the tenuous discontent. She replied with tender understanding. Sometimes the calf was hesitant.
*Eat. (Gently, understandingly, but firmly. [Repetition of picture.])* She bent her head and pushed him carefully over, then held his head down with a gentle pressure of her muzzle. *Eat.*

===Chinese===
吃。（画面：菲尔迪/幼崽四肢着地，啃食平原上的草。）
菲尔迪困惑地瞪着她。草不是食物。他把这条讯息加重地发了过去。
里娅感到了那一点淡淡的抵触。她报以温柔的体谅。幼崽有时候就是会犹豫。
*吃。（温柔地，体谅地，但坚定地。[画面重复。]）* 她低下头，小心翼翼地把他拱翻，再用口鼻轻轻压住他的头往下按。*吃。*

===Original===
Phildee squirmed. He slipped out from under her nose and regained his feet. He looked at the other people, who were staring in puzzlement at Riya and himself.
He felt himself pushed forward again. *Eat.*
Abruptly, he realized the situation. In a culture of herbivores, what food could there be but herbiage? There would be milk, in time, but not for﻿—he probed﻿—months.
In probing, too, he found the visualization of his life with her ready at the surface of Riya’s mind.

===Chinese===
菲尔迪扭动起来。他从她鼻子底下溜出来，重新站好。他看看其余的人族，他们正一脸困惑地盯着里娅和他。
他又感到自己被向前推了一把。*吃。*
他猛然醒悟了处境。在食草动物的文化里，除了草还能有什么食物？奶倒是往后会有，但还得等——他探了探——好几个月。
也是在这一探里，他发现一幅图景正现成地摊在里娅心智的表层——他和她一起生活的图景。

===Original===
There was no shelter on the plain. His fur was all the shelter necessary.
But I don’t have any fur.
In the fall, they would move to the southern range.
Walk? A thousand miles?
He would grow big and strong. In a year, he would be a sire himself.
---

===Chinese===
平原上没有遮蔽。他的皮毛就是全部需要的遮蔽。
可我一点皮毛也没有。
到了秋天，他们就要迁往南边的牧地。
走着去？一千英里？
他会长得又高又壮。一年之后，他自己也该当父亲了。
---

===Original===
His reaction was simple, and practiced. He adjusted his reality concept to Reimannian topology. Not actually, but subjectively, he felt himself beginning to slip Earthward.
Riya stiffened in alarm. The calf was straying. The knowledge was relayed from her mother-centers to the telepathic functions.
Stop. You cannot go there. You must be with your mother. You are not grown. Stop. Stay with me. I will protect you. I love you.
---

===Chinese===
他的反应很简单，而且久经练习。他把现实概念调向黎曼拓扑。并非实际，而是在主观上，他感到自己开始朝地球滑去。
里娅惊恐地绷紧了身子。幼崽在走失。这一认知从她的母性中枢传递到了传心机能。
停下。你不能去那里。你必须待在母亲身边。你还没有长大。停下。陪着我。我会保护你。我爱你。
---

===Original===
The universe shuddered. Phildee adjusted frantically. Cutting through the delicately maintained reality concept was a scrambling, jamming frequency of thought. In terror, he flung himself backward into Riya’s world. Standing completely still, he probed frantically into Riya’s mind.
And found her mind only fumblingly beginning to intellectualize the simple formulization of what her instinctive centers had computed, systematized, and activated before her conscious mind had even begun to doubt that everything was well.
His mind accepted the data, and computed.

===Chinese===
宇宙打了个寒颤。菲尔迪狂乱地调整。一道扰乱、干扰性的思维频率穿透了他那精心维持的现实概念。他在恐惧中猛地把自己向后甩回里娅的世界。他站得纹丝不动，发狂似的探进里娅的心智。
却发现她的心智才刚刚笨拙地开始把那条简单的公式加以理智化——而她的本能中枢，早在她那有意识的头脑还没来得及怀疑一切是否安好之前，就早把它计算好、整理好、启动起来了。
他的心智接收了讯息，开始计算。

===Original===
Handless and voiceless, not so fast afoot in their bulkiness as the weakest month-old calf, the people had long ago evolved the restraints necessary for rearing their children.
If the calf romped and ran, his mother ran beside him, and the calf was not permitted to run faster than she. If a calf strayed from its sleeping mother, it strayed only so far, and then the mother woke﻿—but the calf had already long been held back by the time her intelligence awoke to the straying.
The knowledge and computations were fed in Phildee’s rational centers. The Universe﻿—and Earth﻿—were closed to him. He must remain here.

===Chinese===
没有手，没有声音，身躯庞大，跑起来连最孱弱的满月幼崽都不如，人族却早在远古就进化出了养育儿女所必需的约束手段。
幼崽若是撒欢奔跑，母亲便在他身旁跟着跑，绝不容他跑得比她快。幼崽若是从沉睡的母亲身边走开，也只能走开那么一段，随后母亲就会醒来——可在她的智能惊觉这场走失之前，幼崽早就被约束住很久了。
这些认知和计算结果被送进了菲尔迪的理性中枢。宇宙——连同地球——都对他关闭了。他必须留在这里。

===Original===
But human children could not survive in this environment.
He had to find a solution﻿—instantly.
He clinched his fists, feeling his arm muscles quiver.
His lower lip was pulled into his mouth, and his teeth sank in.
The diagram﻿—the pattern﻿—bigger﻿—stronger﻿—try﻿—try﻿—this is not real﻿—*this* is real: brown earth, white clouds, blue sky﻿—try﻿—mouth full of warm salt﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
可人类的儿童在这种环境里活不下去。
他必须找到一条出路——立刻。
他攥紧双拳，感到胳膊上的肌肉直打颤。
他的下唇被拉进嘴里，牙齿咬了进去。
图式——模式——再大些——再强些——使劲——使劲——那里不是真的——*这*里才是真的：褐色的土地，白色的云，蓝色的天——使劲——满嘴温热的咸味……

===Original===
> F is for Phildee!
> O is for Out!
> R is for Riya!
> T is for Topology!
> H is for happiness and home!

---

===Chinese===
> F 者，菲尔迪也！
> O 者，出去也！
> R 者，里娅也！
> T 者，拓扑也！
> H 者，幸福与家园也！

---

===Original===
Riya shook herself. She stood in the furrows of a plowed field, her eyes vacant with bewilderment. She stared uncomprehendingly at the walls and the radar tower, the concrete shoulders of the air raid bunkers. She saw antiaircraft quick-firers being hastily cranked around and down at her, heard Phildee’s shout that saved her life, and understood none of it.
But none of it mattered. Her strange calf was with her, standing beside her with his fingers locked in her fur, and she could feel the warm response in his mind as she touched him with her caress again.

===Chinese===
里娅抖了抖身子。她站在一片犁过的田垄里，双眼因茫然而空空荡荡。她懵懵懂懂地盯着那些围墙和雷达塔，盯着防空掩体混凝土的肩壁。她看见一门门高射速射炮被慌忙摇着转向她、压低对准她，听见菲尔迪那声救了她性命的喊叫——而这一切，她一样也不懂。
但这一切都无关紧要。她那奇怪的幼崽跟她在一起，手指扣着她的毛，站在她身旁；当她再用爱抚触碰他时，她能感到他心中温暖的回应。

===Original===
She saw the other little calves erupting out of the low dormitory buildings, and something within her crooned.
Riya nuzzled her foundling. She looked about her at the War Orphans’ Relocation Farm with her happy, happy eyes.

===Chinese===
她看见别的小幼崽从低矮的宿舍楼里喷涌而出，她心里有什么在轻轻哼唱。
里娅用口鼻蹭了蹭她的养子。她用那双快乐的、快乐的眼睛，环顾着这座战争孤儿安置农场（War Orphans’ Relocation Farm）。

## Blood on My Jets / 我喷流上的血

### I

===Original===
Rocket Row is the Joy Street of three planets. It’s got neon lights, crummy dives, cheap hotels, and women to match. Every man who’s ever rode a ship into space knows about Rocket Row. It runs along the far side of Flushing Spaceport, down toward the Sound.

The New Shanghai was full of dockworkers and crewmen on liberty. It was noisy. I sat on a bar stool and watched the fog trying to infiltrate the open door. It didn’t have a chance against the tobacco smoke that rolled out to meet it. Outside, the streets and alleys would be choked with wet, creeping darkness, full of quiet footsteps, and the cops would find empty-pocketed corpses behind the ashcans in the morning.

===Chinese===
火箭街（Rocket Row）是三颗行星共有的欢乐街。霓虹灯、破烂的下等酒馆、廉价旅店，还有与之相配的女人。凡是驾船进过太空的男人，没有不知道火箭街的。它沿着法拉盛太空港（Flushing Spaceport）远端一侧延伸，一直通到海峡（the Sound）边。

「新上海」（the New Shanghai）里挤满了码头工人和上岸休假的船员，人声嘈杂。我坐在吧台凳上，看着雾气试图从敞开的门口渗进来。它根本不是对手——滚滚涌出的烟草烟雾迎面就把它顶了回去。门外，街道和小巷想必正被湿漉漉、悄悄蔓延的黑暗堵死，满是轻手轻脚的脚步声；到了早晨，警察准会在垃圾桶后面发现被掏空了口袋的尸体。

---

===Original===
But none of that was any of my business. I was sick and tired of fog﻿—the real kind, the kind they grow on Venus﻿—and I was sick of the thought of blood. I’d seen too much of it, soaking into the hot mud, and some of it spilled by my guns. I wanted to forget the night, and fog that gave cover to every kind of dirty deal a man could imagine. I wanted to pull the corners of my world together until all that was left was the drink, the bar stool, and me. But it wasn’t going to work out that way, because I was in the New Shanghai on business.

And my kind of business was the dirtiest, lousiest, most thankless racket in the world.

The bartender moved up to where I was sitting. “Have another one, Ash?” he asked.

“Yeah, sure, Ming,” I said. “You still make the best Stingers in the System. Maybe that’s because you don’t brew your own gin.”

===Chinese===
可这些都与我无关。我对雾早就烦透了——真正意义上的雾，金星上培育出来的那种——对血这个念头也同样厌烦。我见得太多了，血渗进滚烫的泥浆里，其中有一些就是从我的枪口下淌出来的。我想忘掉黑夜，忘掉那种给人所能想出来的每一种肮脏交易都打了掩护的雾。我想把自己这个世界的四角收拢起来，最后只剩下酒、吧台凳，还有我。但事情不会那样发展，因为我到「新上海」来是办事的。

而我干的这一行，是天下最脏、最下作、最没人领情的营生。

酒保凑到我坐的地方。「再来一杯，阿什（Ash）？」他问。

「好啊，当然，明（Ming）。」我说，「整个太阳系里，还是数你调的施汀格（Stinger）最棒。也许是因为你不用自酿的金酒。」

===Original===
“Could be, Ash, could be,” he laughed. He shook up the drink and poured it in my glass. “How’d it go on Venus?”

“It went,” I said.

Ming was one of the few people who admitted knowing I was a D.O.﻿—a Detached Operative. It was a crummy job, but it suited me.

We were the hired-gun rabble of the System, thrown together into the damnedest police force there had ever been. Spacial expansion hadn’t really gotten underway until after the Terro-Martian War, and after it ended every would-be bigshot there was had realized that all he really needed to set himself up as a pocket-size dictator was some salvaged gear from the mess the war had left, a crew that wasn’t too particular, and a good-looking piece of territory in the practically limitless areas of space. Most of them had picked slices of Venus. There were a few in the Asteroids, hooked up with renegade Marties, and one or two that had actually grabbed sections of Mars.

Sending regular law enforcement officers or Marines after each one of these boys would have been physically impossible. Earth government had come up with a cuter idea.

It was a lot more economical to fight one big decisive battle than to endure a series of inconclusive skirmishes. There were a lot of us boys out in space, most of us just drifting from one port to the next, picking up a living by our wits, and by our skill with a gun, some of us. Earth government had quietly picked out the ones they considered trustworthy, sworn us in, and turned us loose with a few standing orders and a lot of dependence on our discretion.

===Chinese===
「也许吧，阿什，也许吧。」他笑着说。他摇匀酒，倒进我的杯子。「金星那趟活儿怎么样？」

「办完了。」我说。

明是为数不多、肯承认知道我是 D.O.——特派行动员（Detached Operative）——的人。这是份下等活儿，但合我的胃口。

我们是太阳系里受雇拿枪的乌合之众，被拼凑成一支史无前例、最不像话的警察部队。太空开拓直到地火战争（Terro-Martian War）之后才真正铺开；战争一结束，各路自命不凡的大人物都明白过来：要想自立为袖珍独裁者，真正需要的不过是几件从战争烂摊子里捞回来的装备、一伙不太挑剔的人手，再加上太空里那几乎无边无际的区域中一块体面的地盘。他们大多挑了金星的几块切片。小行星带里也有几个，跟叛逃的火星佬勾结在一起，还有一两个真刀真枪占了火星的几段地界。

要对这些家伙一个个派出正规执法官员或海军陆战队，在物理上根本行不通。地球政府想出了一个更妙的主意。

打一场决定性的大仗，比没完没了地忍受一连串不分胜负的小冲突合算得多。太空里有的是我们这种男人，大多数只是从一个港口漂到下一个港口，靠脑子混口饭吃，我们中间有些人，还靠一手好枪法。地球政府悄悄挑出他们认为信得过的人，让我们宣了誓，发几条行动守则就把我们放出去，其余全凭我们自行裁量。

---

===Original===
Whenever something brewed between two of these minor warlords, we’d come flocking in and hire ourselves out to whichever side we felt had slightly more justice. Sometimes we wound up shooting at each other, but you couldn’t even be sure of that, since most of us didn’t know, beyond a guess or two, who the other D.O.’s were. Usually, though, we had enough brains to pick the right side, and we’d make sure that was the one that came out on top.

It was a process of elimination, actually. The warlords were helped to knock each other off until, eventually, those who remained either proved themselves to be strong leaders, which was what frontier planets needed, or else megalomaniacs, in which case it paid to devote a full-scale military campaign to them.

It was a highly informal system, but it had worked. It was tough on us, but it wasn’t any harder than freelance grifting had been. It left an awful lot to personal discretion, and we paid ourselves out of whatever came to hand, but there hadn’t been any big totalitarian regimes lately, either.

“Yeah, I did pretty well,” I repeated.

Ming puckered his mouth and winked. I used to try and figure out how he did it, standing behind his bar all day, never going out, never talking much except to a few people like me. But I knew for sure that he could have told me exactly how much I’d made on that Venus job﻿—and the gimmick I’d pulled to get it past Customs, too.

===Chinese===
每当这类小军阀之间要出什么事，我们就会蜂拥而至，把自己雇给直觉判断稍稍占理的那一方。有时我们到头来会自相残杀，可就连这一点你也说不准，因为除了猜上一两次，我们大多数人都不知道别的 D.O. 是谁。不过，我们通常脑子还够用，能挑中正确的一边，而且会确保赢到最后的正是那一边。

说穿了，那是一场淘汰赛。军阀们被顺水推舟地互相干掉，直到最后，活下来的要么证明自己是强有力的领袖——边疆行星正需要这个——要么就是自大狂，那就不值得客气，给他们来一场全面军事讨伐划得来。

这是一套极不正规的制度，但它管用。对我们来说日子不好过，可也不比单干行骗时更难熬。它把大量事宜都留给个人裁量，报酬也从手边有什么就拿什么里出，但话说回来，近来也没再冒出什么大型极权政权。

「是啊，干得还算不错。」我又说了一遍。

明抿了抿嘴，眨了眨眼。我以前总想弄明白他是怎么打听到那些的——整天站在吧台后面，从不出去，除了对像我这样的少数几个人，话也不多。但我确信，他连我在金星那趟活儿上挣了多少钱都能分毫不差地说出来——还有我把货混过海关用的什么花招，他也一清二楚。

===Original===
But that was why I was in here. Something was up﻿—something big, and I wanted to find out what it was before every grifter and chiseler in the System tried to cut a piece of it for himself.

“I got a note in my mailbox today,” I said casually.

“Yeah?” he asked, just as quietly.

“Must have been put there as soon as I touched down this morning. Somebody wants me to go to work for them. They’re paying high﻿—too high, maybe. Hear anything about a big job coming off somewhere?”

Ming grinned. “If you mean that little letter from Transolar, yeah, I know about that.” He got serious, and moved closer.

“But that’s all I know, and nobody else knows even that much. Sure, something’s cooking, but nobody knows what it is. I﻿—” He broke off. “You’ve got company. Boy, *have* you got company!”

===Chinese===
但我正是为这个才来的。有什么事正在酝酿——是大事，我要赶在全太阳系的每个骗子和小油子都想着从中切一块之前，弄清它到底是什么。

「今天我的信箱里收到张字条。」我随口说。

「哦？」他问，同样轻描淡写。

「准是我今天早上刚落地那会儿塞进去的。有人要我替他们做事。出的价很高——没准高得过头了。听说哪儿有什么大买卖要开张吗？」

明咧嘴一笑。「你要是说特兰索拉（Transolar）那封小信，对，那个我知道。」他神色一正，凑近了些。

「但我知道的就这么多，别人连这点都不知道。的确，有事在酝酿，可没人知道是什么。我——」他忽然打住。「你有客人来了。乖乖，你这客人可真够瞧的！」

===Original===
I looked in the backbar mirror. A girl had come in the doorway and was walking toward me. Her dress tightened in intriguing places. Her face was as much of a treat. High-cheeked, brown-eyed, with a small, uptilted nose and a full mouth, it was framed by short curly hair the color of new copper wire. I liked it.

So did the spacemen and the dockworkers sitting at the bar. One or two half-rose to invite her to join them, but they sat down again when they saw who she was headed for.

There was something about that hair. I’d seen it before, somewhere.

The guy next to me got up and slid out of the way. I let my eyes stay on the bottles on the backbar until she sat down beside me. I gave Ming a look. He nodded, and moved down the bar.

“Ash?”

===Chinese===
我朝吧台后面的镜子望去。一个姑娘出现在门口，正朝我走来。她的裙子在引人遐想的地方绷得紧紧的。她的脸同样耐看。高颧骨，棕色的眼睛，小巧上翘的鼻子，饱满的嘴唇，一头短短的卷发框住脸庞，颜色像新拉的铜丝。我喜欢这张脸。

坐在吧台前的宇航员和码头工人们也喜欢。有一两个半站起来想请她过去坐，可一看清她冲谁来，又坐了回去。

那头头发有点眼熟。我从前在哪儿见过。

我旁边的家伙起身让开了。我让目光一直停在吧台后面的酒瓶上，直到她在我身旁坐下。我朝明递了个眼色。他点点头，沿吧台挪开了。

「阿什？」

===Original===
The voice was low, but crisp. It had whispers and murmurs in it, too, and I knew I’d heard it before.

“I’m Pat McKay.”

I turned my head and looked at her. Her dress, tight as paint from hem to bodice, was mysteriously loose in the sleeves. Ruffles at each shoulder hid bulges that Mother Nature never put there. They looked more like twin shoulder holsters. They were.

===Chinese===
嗓音低沉，却干脆利落。里面还带着些耳语般的轻柔和呢喃，我知道这声音从前听过。

「我是帕特·麦凯（Pat McKay）。」

我转过头看她。她那条裙子从裙摆到胸衣紧得像刷上去的漆，两只袖子却蹊跷地宽松。两肩的褶边下鼓起一团团东西，那可不是老天爷赐给女人的。看着倒更像一对肩挂枪套。事实上正是。

---

===Original===
And the last time I’d seen her, she was seventeen﻿—eighteen, maybe﻿—in a ball gown, her hair long then, curling around her shoulders.

And the voice hadn’t been as controlled, or as crisp, but she’d been saying, “You’re a good dancer, Mr. Holcomb. Not much on the light conversation, but a good leader.”

I’d swept her around another couple, and kept my cheek away from hers. “The Academy is geared to the production of good leaders, Pat. Good conversationalists, on the other hand, are born, not made.”

She laughed﻿—a giddy party laugh from a girl who dated Academy boys exclusively, who loved the glitter and pomp of graduation ceremonies, who hung around the Academy all she could, who had been to Graduation Balls before, and would certainly be to a number of them again, before she managed to separate all the black and silver uniforms she’d danced with and found herself a man from inside one of them. An Academy drag﻿—a number in a score of little black books.

“Like Harry﻿—oh, pardon me, it’s Graduation Night﻿—like Mr. Thorsten, you mean?” And she looked up at me, raking my face with her green eyes.

“If you will.”

===Chinese===
而我上次见到她时，她才十七岁——也许十八——穿着舞会礼服，那时她的头发还长，卷曲着垂在肩头。

那时她的嗓音还不像现在这样收放自如、干脆利落，但她说的话是：「你舞跳得不错，霍尔科姆（Holcomb）先生。闲聊差了点，但是个好领舞的。」

我带她旋过另一对舞伴，脸颊始终避着她的。「学院（the Academy）就是为培养好领袖而设的，帕特。至于好的闲聊家，那是天生的，不是造出来的。」

她笑了——一种晕乎乎的宴会笑，出自这样一个姑娘：只跟学院的小伙子约会，迷恋毕业典礼的流光与排场，能泡在学院里多久就泡多久；她以前就参加过毕业舞会，往后肯定还会再赴一场又一场，直到把跟她跳过舞的那些黑银色制服一一分辨清楚，从其中一身里给自己挑出个男人来。学院丫头一个——几十本小黑簿子里记着的一个号码。

「像哈里（Harry）那样——哦，对不起，今儿是毕业之夜——像索斯滕（Thorsten）先生那样，你是说？」她抬起头望我，那双绿眼睛像耙子一样刮过我的脸。

「你要这么理解也行。」

===Original===
“You’re jealous, Mr. Holcomb,” she said, breaking out her best little tease manner.

“Maybe.” I knew she was trying to get me angry. She was getting there fast, too.

“Well, now, if you displayed some of Mr. Thorsten’s other gifts, I could forget about the conversation,” she said lightly.

“Meaning you’d like me to dance you out on the terrace and make a pass at you?”

“Maybe.”

She was daring me.

===Chinese===
「你吃醋了，霍尔科姆先生。」她说，拿出她最拿手的那套小撩拨做派。

「也许吧。」我知道她在设法惹我动气。而且她这一招见效很快。

「唔，这么说吧，你要是能露两手索斯滕先生别的本事，聊天差点我也可以不计较。」她轻快地说。

「意思是，你想让我把你舞到露台上去，对你动手动脚？」

「也许吧。」

她在将我的军。

===Original===
I danced her out on the terrace, and found a darker corner. She looked up at me, her eyes a little surprised, but her lips were parted.

I tightened my arms and kissed her. It started gently﻿—just a kiss sneaked in between dances﻿—but her arms were growing tighter too, and her fingers were hooking. We held it, while I listened to the blood running in my ears, until we broke apart, both of us dropping our arms, standing and looking at each other, dragging air down our throats.

“Ash! You﻿—”

She started to say something, and broke it. It sounded a little too much like a movie heroine, all of a sudden. She was holding the pose a little too long, too. “Hell, she’s a kid﻿—she’s doing it the way the grownups in the movies do it,” I told myself, but I’d danced her out here for a purpose. Maybe she didn’t deserve it, but I was sick to death of the little bits of fluff that hung around, drinking in borrowed glamor, getting the big play from boys like Harry Thorsten.

I reached out and grabbed.

===Chinese===
我把她舞到了露台上，寻了个更暗的角落。她抬眼望我，眼神有点意外，嘴唇却微微张开。

我收紧双臂，吻了她。起初很轻——不过是趁舞步间隙偷来的一吻——可她的手臂也越收越紧，手指扣了上来。我们维持着那个吻，我听着热血在耳朵里奔涌，直到我们分开，两人都垂下手，站在那里对望着，喉咙里火辣辣地往里吸气。

「阿什！你——」

她开口想说什么，又咽了回去。那腔调忽然听着太像电影里的女主角了。那个姿态她也端得久了点。「见鬼，她还是个孩子——在学电影里大人的样子呢。」我对自己说，但我把她舞到这儿来是有目的的。也许她罪不至此，可我对那些到处晃悠、啜饮着借来的光彩、从哈里·索斯滕这类小子那里捞足风头的轻浮丫头，早就恶心透了。

我伸手抓住了她。

===Original===
“Now comes the part you’ve really been asking for,” I said. I crouched, bent her over my knee, and brought my hand down. Hard. Three times in all, putting everything I had into it.

“Now,” I said, letting her get up, “maybe you’ll quit bothering guys who worked all their lives to get in a spot where they could go out and be of some help in the only job they ever wanted﻿—the T.S.N. Do you think you really stack up worth a damn beside the only thing that counts?”

She just stood there, tears of rage in her eyes. I was never sure whether it was what I’d done or what I said that had her so mad, but the last thing I heard her say as I walked away was: “Damn you, Ash Holcomb! Damn you for being such a snobbish stuck-up.﻿ ﻿…”

Well, maybe I was wrong and maybe I wasn’t. I didn’t know as much in those days as I should have, either. But it was too late now﻿—too late by a war and a hundred revolutions, too late by all the men who’d gone down before my guns, too late by years of loneliness and bitterness.

But if it was too late, why did I remember it all now, with Thorsten up in the Asteroids, a little king in his own right, with me in the New Shanghai, a white ray-burn splashed through my hair, with the Academy a dim thing behind both of us, and Pat﻿—

Why was Pat here? What had she done through the years, while I fought my way from one end of the System to the other, and Harry took the easy way out during the war?

===Chinese===
「现在来你真正盼着的那一段。」我说。我蹲下身，把她按翻在膝盖上，手掌落了下去。狠狠地。总共三下，每一下都用足了力气。

「好了，」我松手让她起来，「也许你该放过那些家伙了——他们奋斗了一辈子，才挣到这个位置，能出去干他们唯一想干的事业，出一份力——T.S.N.。你掂量掂量，跟这唯一要紧的事业比，你真有半点分量吗？」

她只是站在那儿，眼里噙着愤怒的泪。我始终没弄清，惹得她那样暴怒的，究竟是我做的事还是我说的话，但我走开时听到她的最后一句话是：「该死的，阿什·霍尔科姆！你傲慢自负得这么势利眼，真该死。……」

唉，也许我错了，也许我没错。那些日子我该懂的事，懂得也还不够多。可现在都太迟了——隔了一场战争和上百场革命那么迟，隔了倒在我枪下的那么多人那么迟，隔了多年孤独与怨愤那么迟。

可既然都太迟了，为什么我现在偏偏全都想了起来——索斯滕在小行星带（the Asteroids）上，自立门户当了小国王；我坐在「新上海」里，头发间横着一道白色射线灼痕；学院在我们两人身后都已黯淡——还有帕特——

帕特为什么会在这里？这些年，我从太阳系这头一路厮杀到那头，哈里在战争里挑了条省力的路——帕特又做了些什么？

---

===Original===
“Hello, Pat,” I said. “I haven’t seen you in a long time.” Well, what else was I going to say?

I don’t know what she had expected me to say. She kept her face in profile, and didn’t let me see what it was showing.

“I’m here on business. I hear you’re a good man, these days, for the job I’ve got.” She twisted the words like a knife.

All right, if she wanted it that way, she’d get it.

“So they tell me,” I said.

“Fifteen thousand for a month’s work.”

===Chinese===
「你好，帕特。」我说，「好久不见了。」还能说什么呢？

我不知道她料想我会说什么。她始终侧着脸，不让我看清脸上流露的是什么。

「我来这里是谈生意的。听说这些日子，你干我手上这活儿是把好手。」她把每个字都拧得像刀子似的。

好啊，她要来这一套，那就奉陪。

「他们也是这么说的。」我说。

「干一个月，一万五。」

===Original===
She said it quietly, without any buildup. Maybe she figured fifteen thousand didn’t need one.

I sat there for a minute, not saying anything, but thinking hard. What kind of a setup was she offering me? Was this the big job that was floating around? There’s usually a sure way to find out. When someone offers you a blind deal, argue. Maybe they’ll get mad, or scared you won’t take it, and spill something.

“No, thanks,” I said.

She frowned. “Don’t try haggling with me, Ash. I can get somebody just as good for less.”

“I don’t doubt it. You could probably get three. That’s why I don’t want any part of it. It’s sucker bait.”

She looked at me for the first time, mouth twisted.

===Chinese===
她说得很平静，没做半点铺垫。也许她觉得，一万五这个数用不着铺垫。

我坐了一会儿，什么也没说，脑子却转得飞快。她递给我的是个什么局？外头风传的那件大买卖就是这个吗？要摸底通常有个稳当的法子：有人塞给你一桩不明底细的买卖时，就跟他还价。没准他会恼了，或者怕你不接，漏出点口风。

「不接，谢了。」我说。

她皱起眉。「别跟我讨价还价，阿什。少花点钱，我照样能找到一样好的人手。」

「这我信。你多半能找到三个。所以我才不想沾边。那是给冤大头下的饵。」

她第一次正眼看我，嘴角扭着。

===Original===
“Since when does a hired gun like you turn down that kind of money? The job’s worth it, believe me.”

That hit me. But I couldn’t afford to get touchy.

“Probably is. But with standard pay at three thousand a month, plus bounties and commissions, this little errand of yours, whatever it may be, must break so many laws it could land me in a death house,” I said, watching her eyes.

It didn’t add up. Nothing added up. Why had she picked me, in the first place? I had a reputation as one of the better gunnies, sure, but there were at least twenty guys I’d never draw against, if I could help it, and four or five of them were available. Because she’d known me? And this job﻿—what kind of hanky-panky was going on at these prices?

I watched her eyes acquiring dangerous highlights. The temper that went with that hair was beginning to stir.

“Do you want to get in on the biggest deal that’s ever been pulled off in space or don’t you?” she said. “Or are you going to chicken out?” she added contemptuously.

===Chinese===
「像你这种吃枪把子饭的，什么时候也跟这种钱过不去了？这活儿值这个价，信我。」

这话戳到了我。可我不能动气。

「多半是值。可标准行情一个月三千，外加赏金和佣金——你这件小差事，不管内容是什么，犯的法律肯定多到够把我送进死囚牢。」我说，一边盯着她的眼睛。

账对不上。什么都不对。首先，她为什么挑中我？没错，我算得上有名的好枪手之一，可这行里至少有二十个人，只要能躲开我就绝不跟他们拔枪相对，其中四五个眼下就有空。因为我跟她认识？还有这活儿——这种价钱背后，藏着什么猫腻？

我看着她的眼里泛起危险的光。配得上那头头发的脾气，开始动了。

「太空里有史以来最大的一票买卖，你到底想不想入伙？」她说，又轻蔑地加了一句：「还是说，你要当缩头乌龟？」

===Original===
I let it slide off my shoulders.

“I don’t know,” I said. I wanted to get a chance to really talk things out with her, and this wasn’t the place for it. “Anyway, this is no place to talk business. Walk out of here as if I’d turned you down, and go up the street. I’ll catch up to you.”

“Okay.” She got up and walked out.

“Sorry, Honey,” I called after her, loud enough for everybody to hear. A snicker went up. I cut it off with a look at the characters lined up against the bar, and got back to my drink. I finished it casually, put it down, paid, and walked slowly to the door. I let everybody get a good look at me turning down the street in the opposite direction from the one Pat had taken.

I ducked into the first cross street and moved swiftly over to the alley that paralleled the street that Pat was on. I was thinking all the way.

Being a D.O. was one thing﻿—getting into something solo was another. I could get killed, for all I knew, and maybe by a lawman’s gun. That was a risk I ran on every job, but in this case, I didn’t even know, yet, what was going on. The smart thing to do would have been to pass the word to my S.B.I. contact, but that would take too much time. There was nothing I could do but dive into this mess head-on, and hope I’d have time to yell for help later.

===Chinese===
我没接这个茬。

「说不准。」我说。我想找机会跟她真正把话摊开谈清楚，而这里不是地方。「反正，这儿不是谈生意的地方。你走出去，就当我回绝了你，然后往街那头走。我会追上你。」

「好。」她起身走了出去。

「抱歉了，宝贝儿。」我在她身后喊道，声音大到人人听得见。一阵哄笑了起来。我朝吧台边排着的那些家伙扫了一眼，把笑声压了下去，然后接着喝我的酒。我不紧不慢地喝完，放下杯子，付了账，慢步走向门口。我让所有人都看清楚，我拐进的那条街，和帕特走的方向正好相反。

我闪进第一条横街，快步绕到与帕特所在那条街平行的小巷里。一路上我都在盘算。

当 D.O. 是一回事——单枪匹马蹚进来是另一回事。弄不好我会送命，而且没准死在执法官的枪下。这种风险每接一单活儿都有，可眼下这桩，我甚至连出了什么事都还不知道。按聪明的做法，该先把消息递给我的 S.B.I. 联络人，但那太费时间。我别无选择，只能一头扎进这摊浑水，指望之后还来得及呼救。

===Original===
I was about to turn into another alley that ran back to the main street when I heard the coughing of a Saro airgun and the faint sizzle of a Colt in reply.

Instantly, I was running silently up the alley. One hand unzipped the chest of my coverall, and the other one dove in and grabbed the butt of the heavy Sturmey that’s my favorite man-killer. I reached the mouth of the alley and stopped abruptly in the shadows.

A man lay in the middle of the street, unnaturally flat against the concrete slab. The street lamp up the block was dark, its base surrounded by shattered glass.

The Saro went into action again from the roof of a building across the street. I saw the slugs chip cement from the railing of a flight of steps four doors up. A pale blue flare winked from behind the railing, and the man with the Saro ducked, but was up again as another gun raked the stairs from a spot on my side of the street. I didn’t like that setup one bit.

The Sturmey in my hand went *whoomp!* and the man on the roof sailed out over the street and landed with a crunch. The other gun cut off abruptly. Two Colt beams probed for it from the stairs, and that clinched it. It was Pat, all right, and somewhere, she’d become a fair hand at street fighting.

“Hey, Pat!” I yelled, and ducked away from the storm of bullets the other gunman flung at me. The result was what I’d hoped for. The man had exposed himself to Pat’s fire by shooting at me. The Colts sizzled viciously, and the burst of Saro noise stopped in mid-clip.

===Chinese===
我正要拐进另一条通回大街的小巷，忽然听见一支萨罗（Saro）气枪的咳嗽声，和一支柯尔特（Colt）还击的轻微嘶响。

我立刻悄无声息地沿小巷奔去。一只手拉开连体工作服的胸襟，另一只手伸进去，攥住那支沉甸甸的斯特米（Sturmey）的枪柄——那是我最爱用的一件杀人利器。我奔到巷口，猛地停在阴影里。

一个男人趴在街心，紧贴着混凝土路面，扁平得不像活人。街那头的路灯黑着，灯座四周碎玻璃散了一地。

那支萨罗又从街对面一栋楼顶上开火了。我看见弹头把四户开外一段台阶栏杆上的水泥削得屑子乱飞。栏杆后面闪起一点淡蓝色的火光，持萨罗的人缩了下头，但随着另一支枪从我这一侧街面的某个位置扫射台阶，他又冒了起来。这个局面我一万个不喜欢。

我手里的斯特米「咚！」地一响，楼顶上那人腾空飞出，横过街道，落地时咔嚓一声闷响。另一支枪戛然而止。两道柯尔特光束从台阶那边探过去搜寻，这一下确定了。是帕特，没错，而且不知在哪儿，她练出了一手过得硬的巷战功夫。

「嘿，帕特！」我大喊一声，闪身躲开另一个枪手泼来的弹雨。效果正合我意。那人朝我开枪，就把自己暴露给了帕特的火力。柯尔特恶狠狠地嘶啸起来，萨罗的连发声在弹夹打到一半时就断了。

===Original===
A gun clattered on cement. I poked my head cautiously around the corner. Silence blanketed Rocket Row, and then was tempered by a scuffing noise. Up the street, a leather belt was being pressed against the side of a building by the weight of a body that was sliding slowly downwards. I spotted a glowing dot that was a tunic smoldering around a Colt burn.

“Ash!”

“Yeah?”

“You okay?”

I grinned. She sounded a little worried.

===Chinese===
一支枪当啷一声砸在水泥地上。我小心翼翼地从墙角探出头。寂静罩住了火箭街，随后被一阵擦刮声打破。街上远处，一条皮带紧贴着楼墙，被一具缓缓下滑的尸身的重量坠着。我看见一个发亮的红点——那是柯尔特灼伤处闷烧的制服布料。

「阿什！」

「怎么了？」

「你没事吧？」

我咧嘴一笑。她的语气有点发急。

===Original===
I sprinted across the street at a weaving run, and dove behind the stairway.

“What happened?” I asked.

“I don’t know﻿—but I’ve got an idea. I got about a hundred yards up the street when I spotted this guy tailing me. I yelled, and he ducked. At the same time, this other fellow started running toward me across the street. I burned him down, and ducked in here just as the bird on the roof opened up. That’s it, until you came along.”

I swore. I didn’t go for three men gunning one girl. I looked over the top of the railing. One or two people were starting to come out of doorways.

“Maybe we’d better get out of here,” I said.

===Chinese===
我左绕右晃地疾冲过街，翻滚着躲到台阶后面。

「怎么回事？」我问。

「不知道——不过我有数。我沿街走出一百来码，就发现这家伙在盯我的梢。我喊了一声，他缩了回去。同时，另一个家伙开始穿过街朝我跑来。我放倒了他，刚闪进这儿，楼顶上那家伙就开了火。就这些，直到你露面。」

我骂了一句。三个人拿枪围攻一个姑娘，这种事我看不惯。我从栏杆顶上望出去。已有一两个人开始从门洞里探头。

「我们最好离开这儿。」我说。

---

===Original===
We ran up the street to another alley. She re-holstered her guns on the way, revealing a lot of what the dress advertised.

We stopped inside the alley and caught our breaths. “Well, anyway,” I said, “I know what you’re in this for.”

She looked up sharply. “What?”

“You need money to buy some underwear with.”

She slammed her hand into my face. I ducked back, and stood there, blinking.

“Look, Holcomb, as far as I’m concerned, the deal’s on. Fine. Thanks for helping me out back there, too. But just thanks﻿—no further payment. And no kidding around. This is a business deal. Have you got that straight, or do I burn you down where you stand and find another boy?”

===Chinese===
我们沿街跑进另一条小巷。一路上她把枪插回枪套，也把她那条裙子大做广告的东西露了个七七八八。

我们在巷子里停下，喘匀了气。「好了，不管怎么说，」我开口，「我知道你图这个什么了。」

她猛地抬头。「图什么？」

「你缺钱买内衣穿。」

她一巴掌扇在我脸上。我缩头躲开，站在那儿直眨眼。

「听着，霍尔科姆，就我而言，买卖成立，很好。多谢你刚才帮我解了围。但只是道谢——没有别的报答。也别耍嘴皮子。这是一桩生意。你给我记牢了，还是我现在就把你撂倒在原地，另找个男人？」

===Original===
She meant it. I looked down at her hand, and one of the Colts was in it.

“Okay.” I hadn’t meant that crack as a pass, but as long as the question had come up, it was all right by me to have it settled right here. “But put that thing away before I make you eat it.”

She grinned, suddenly, and put the gun back. “I’m sorry, Ash. But it’s the best way I’ve ever found to establish a clear-cut business relationship. Partners?”

She stuck out her hand, and I took it.

“Deal.”

A siren rose and died on Rocket Row. Pat jumped back. “Damn it!” she said. She shot a glance up the alley. “We’d better split up,” she said. “Look, Ash,” she said hastily, “I’ll get in touch with you. Meanwhile, do what I tell you to, and don’t waste time asking me why. I’ll tell you later. All you have to do now is take the job Transolar is going to offer you. That’s all. Take that job, and start to carry it out. I’ll be in touch with you somewhere along the line.”

===Chinese===
她说到做到。我低头看她的手，一支柯尔特正握在里面。

「行。」我那句俏皮话本没有调情的意思，不过既然话赶话到了这儿，就地把它说清楚我也没意见。「不过先把那玩意儿收起来，不然我让你把它吃下去。」

她忽然咧嘴一笑，把枪收了回去。「对不住，阿什。但这是我发现的最能确立泾渭分明的生意关系的法子。合伙？」

她伸出手，我握住了。

「成交。」

一声警笛在火箭街上响起，又低落下去。帕特缩了一下。「见鬼！」她说。她朝巷子上方飞快瞥了一眼。「我们最好分开走。」她说。「听好，阿什，」她急急地说，「我会跟你联系。这几天，我让你做什么你就做什么，别浪费时间问我为什么。我以后会告诉你的。你现在要做的只有一件事：接下特兰索拉要给你的那份活儿。就这些。接下它，然后开始执行。路上某处我会再找你。」

===Original===
She looked down toward the alley’s mouth. I followed her glance, and saw shadowy figures of men running by.

“They’ll be in here in a minute. I’ve got a car a couple of blocks away. I’ll see you, Ash.”

“Yeah. Hurry up,” I added, as the first of the cops came warily into the alley.

I pulled my gun and ducked behind a barrel as she started to run. The cop yelled and came after her. I snapped a shot over his head, and that drove him into cover. Over the shouts that rose, I could hear her footsteps fading out.

I followed her cautiously, sliding from behind one ashcan to another, keeping the cops down with an occasional shot. I made it out of the alley and into the street, then ducked into a doorway, kicked the lock loose, took the stairs two at a time to the roof, and got away over the housetops.

And all the time, I was wondering about Pat, the job that Transolar was going to offer me, and how she’d known about it.

===Chinese===
她朝巷口望去。我顺着她的目光，看见几条人影跑过。

「他们一分钟内就会摸进来。我的车停在两个街区外。回头见，阿什。」

「好。快走。」我补了一句，第一批警察已经警觉地摸进巷子。

她刚起跑，我便拔枪闪到一只木桶后面。那警察大喊着追她。我朝他头顶崩了一枪，把他逼进了掩体。在四起的喊声里，我能听见她的脚步声渐渐远去。

我小心地跟着她，从一个垃圾桶后面滑到下一个，不时放一枪压住警察。我总算出了巷子上了街，随即闪进一个门洞，一脚踹开门锁，两级一跨奔上屋顶，越过屋脊脱了身。

而这一路，我心里一直在嘀咕帕特，嘀咕特兰索拉就要给我的那份活儿，还有——她怎么会知道它的。
### II

===Original===
Mort Weidmann was the same Captain Weidmann who’d left an arm in the cockpit of a K class scoutbomber that he’d flown through a formation of Marties while he almost bled to death. He looked very military in his blue and silver uniform. It wasn’t a T.S.N. uniform, of course, but even a Transolar Express rig makes an old soldier feel better.

He was another old friend of mine, like Thorsten. The three of us had been touched by the war, each in our separate ways. Mort was the one who didn’t just feel a yearning for space, who didn’t just ride on a T.S.N. uniform because it was the one available way. Mort had loved the T.S.N. itself, with a pride in the traditions that guys like Thorsten and me hadn’t quite had. He’d been a better officer because of it﻿—and the only one who couldn’t have stayed.

And, as we’d gone our separate ways, so our ways of thinking had changed. Thorsten﻿—well, he’d taken his choice, and some day I might have to go into the Belt and do something about it, but Mort’s attitude hurt. He didn’t have any respect for me﻿—he couldn’t have, for a man who’d resigned his commission and become a planet-hopper.

He stood at the window in his office, his phony arm tucked into a pocket, his moustache moving up and down as he talked to me.

“I don’t know why they picked you, Ash,” he said.

I leaned back in my chair. “I don’t either﻿—unless maybe it’s because they couldn’t find anybody else with my qualifications. Or maybe it’s because they can trust me, and they know it.” I was getting pretty mad. Weidmann was a right guy, but I was getting sick of being offered jobs without being told what they were. Two in two days was a little too much.

===Chinese===
莫特·魏德曼（Mort Weidmann）就是当年那位魏德曼上尉——他驾着一架 K class 侦察轰炸机硬闯火星佬的编队，几乎流血流到死，一条胳膊永远留在了座舱里。他穿一身蓝银相间的制服，很有军人气派。那自然不是 T.S.N. 的制服，但哪怕一身特兰索拉快运的行头，也能让老兵心里好受些。

他和索斯滕一样，也是我的老朋友。我们三个都被那场战争触动过，各自以各自的方式。莫特不是那种仅仅怀着对太空的渴望、或者仅仅因为 T.S.N. 制服是唯一现成出路才穿上它的人。莫特爱的是 T.S.N. 本身，怀着对它传统的自豪——那是索斯滕和我这类人都不曾有过的。正因如此，他曾是更出色的军官——也是唯一不可能留下来的人。

而我们各奔前程之后，想法也随之变了。索斯滕——唉，他做了他的选择，早晚有一天我也许得进小行星带去会会他。但莫特的态度最伤人。他对我毫无敬意——也不可能有，对着一个辞去军职、当了星际浪人的家伙。

他站在办公室的窗前，那条假臂插在口袋里，跟我说话时胡子一翘一翘。

「我不明白他们为什么挑中你，阿什。」他说。

我往椅背上一靠。「我也不明白——除非是因为，他们再也找不出第二个有我这般资历的人。要么，就是他们信得过我，而且知道这一点。」我火气越蹿越高。魏德曼是个正派人，可这种活儿不明不白就往你怀里塞的滋味，我已经受够了。两天两桩，未免太多。

===Original===
Weidmann turned around. “Don’t get edgy, Ash! I’ve got my orders﻿—they came down from the top brass, and I’ll carry them, whether I approve or not. But don’t get me sore. I’m authorized to offer you ten thousand dollars, plus expenses, for one trip to Titan and back. You’ll be carrying extremely valuable cargo, and you’ll be expected to deliver it intact. Do you want the job, or not?”

I didn’t answer him right away. What was wrong with him? There was more than just dislike riding his voice.

“I don’t get,” I stalled. “Like you’ve said, why me? And why Titan? There’s nothing out there. Besides, the Asteroid Belt is full of Marties, to say nothing of Thorsten and his crew. Nobody in his right mind would try to make that trip without a convoy.”

Weidmann flushed. “For your information,” he said, “there’s a small scientific staff in a bubble on Titan. They need a new charge for their power pile, and we’ve got the shipping contract. Our problem is to get it to them without Thorsten or the Martians learning about it and grabbing it up. That’s why we dug you up. We need somebody who can fly it out to them and fight off raiders at the same time. You’re still the best available.”

So that was the big job! No wonder there were so many phony things going on!

“For God, for Country, and for Transolar, huh?” I said, watching the blood leave his face. “Now why should I help you pull your fat contracts out of the fire? What’s it to me if a bunch of technicians don’t get their damn fuel? The stuff’d be worth plenty to either Thorsten or the Marties. Living in the Asteroids isn’t fun﻿—I’ve done it, and it takes power to maintain a bubble. Believe me, they’ll throw everything they’ve got to keep a ship carrying a pile charge from making it past them.”

===Chinese===
魏德曼转过身来。「别急赤白脸的，阿什！我有我的命令——最高层直接下达的，不管我赞不赞成，都得执行。可也别惹我冒火。我受权向你开价：去泰坦（Titan）跑一趟再回来，一万美元，外加开销。你要运的货物极其贵重，而且必须完好无损地送到。这活儿你接不接？」

我没有立刻答话。他这是怎么了？他话音里骑着的，可不只是不喜欢。

「我想不通。」我拖延着。「就像你说的，为什么是我？又为什么是泰坦？那鬼地方什么都没有。再说，小行星带里到处是火星佬，更别提索斯滕和他那伙人了。脑子正常的人，没有护航队绝不会去跑那一趟。」

魏德曼涨红了脸。「告诉你也无妨，」他说，「泰坦上有个气泡舱，里面驻着一小队科研人员。他们的动力堆需要换新的堆芯燃料，运输合同在我们手里。我们的难题，是要把东西送到他们那儿，还不能让索斯滕或者火星人察觉、截走。所以才把你挖了出来。我们需要一个能把货送到、同时还能打退劫掠者的人。眼下能找到的，还是你最好。」

原来那就是那件大买卖！难怪有这么多猫腻！

「为了上帝，为了国家，也为了特兰索拉，嗯？」我说，眼看着血色从他脸上褪下去。「那我为什么要帮你们把肥合同从火里抢出来？一帮技术员拿不到他们那点破燃料，关我什么事？这玩意儿不管落到索斯滕还是火星佬手里都值大钱。在小行星带里过活可不是闹着玩的——我待过，维持一个气泡舱全靠能源。信我的，为了让一艘载着堆芯燃料的飞船过不去，他们会把手头的一切都押上来。」

===Original===
I must have sounded pretty nasty about it, because Weidmann actually yanked that murderous motorized artificial arm out of his pocket. He pulled up his shoulders and looked at me like I was something floating down a sewer, but he kept his voice even.

“All right, Ash. Ten thousand, plus expenses. You’ll be given a new kind of ship. It’s a model we picked up from a manufacturer who had his contract cancelled by the T.S.N. She was originally designed for armed reconnaissance, and we’ve installed the weapons called for in the original specifications. She’ll outfly anything with jets on it, and stand off a cruiser, given room to maneuver. Does that soothe you, or do you want a convoy, too?” he added scornfully.

I lit a cigarette and pretended to think it over. Actually, of course, I was going to take the job. I would have, anyway, but there were two additional reasons why I wouldn’t turn it down. There was Pat, of course, and her orders. Most important though, had been the fact that the message to report to Weidmann that I’d found in my mailbox at the Spacemen’s Hiring Hall had borne a slightly different Post Office cancellation on the stamp than the usual. The *T* in United wasn’t quite formed the way it was on the regular stamp. It wasn’t apparent unless you looked for it﻿—but it was as good as a big red sign that spelled out “Official United Terrestrial Government Business﻿—Act as Directed Within,” because that was what it meant.

“Sounds better than I expected,” I admitted. “All right. When do I go?”

Weidmann didn’t show any expression to indicate disappointment or satisfaction. He simply said, “Tonight, after we check over the details. The ship’s equipped with standard T.S.N. controls, and you’ll have lots of time to test her flight characteristics once you get out in space.”

===Chinese===
我那些话想必说得够难听，因为魏德曼竟把那条杀气腾腾的机动假臂从口袋里猛地抽了出来。他耸起肩膀，看我的眼神就像看顺着下水道漂下来的什么东西，但话音仍保持平稳。

「好吧，阿什。一万，外加开销。会给你配一艘新式的船。那是个我们从一家制造商手里接来的型号——他们的合同被 T.S.N. 砍掉了。她原本是为武装侦察设计的，原规格书里要求的武器我们全都装上了。天上凡带喷流的，她都能甩下；只要有回旋余地，连巡洋舰也能顶住。这下你顺气了，还是连护航队也要？」他轻蔑地补了一句。

我点了支烟，装作掂量。当然，实际上我本来就打算接。就算没有别的，我也接，何况还有两条不推辞的理由。一是帕特和她的指令。而最要紧的一条是：我在宇航员雇工大厅信箱里发现的那张叫我去魏德曼处报到的通知，邮票上的邮戳跟寻常的略有不同。United 里的那个 T，字形与正规邮票上的不太一样。不刻意去找根本看不出来——但它无异于一块大红招牌，上头写着「地球联合政府公务——遵函内指示行事」，因为它的意思正是如此。

「听着比我预想的强。」我承认，「行。什么时候动身？」

魏德曼脸上没露出失望或满意的任何表情。他只说：「今晚，等我们核对完细节。船装的是 T.S.N. 标准操纵系统，等你进了太空，有的是时间摸清她的飞行特性。」

===Original===
“What happens if she explodes? Don’t I get to test her first?”

“No﻿—there isn’t time, and it would be a dead giveaway.” For the first time, I saw something like satisfaction on Weidmann’s face. “And if she explodes﻿ ﻿… well, frankly, Holcomb, that’s your problem.”

===Chinese===
「那要是她炸了呢？我不能先试飞一下？」

「不能——没时间了，而且一试飞就全露馅了。」我头一回在魏德曼脸上看到近似满意的表情。「至于她要是炸了……嗯，坦率讲，霍尔科姆，那是你的问题。」

---

===Original===
I spent the afternoon being briefed. One thing was off my mind﻿—if I had official orders to take this job, then the S.B.I. would be keeping a tab on me. It made a difference, knowing that no matter what kind of a mess I got into, somebody would at least know what had happened to me, and, most important, why.

I was given a Company flight suit, and a hip rig for my Sturmey. I put those on, and was taken to within a block of the port in a shuttered car.

Not going all the way to the spaceport was my idea. The reason I gave Weidmann was good enough﻿—there was no sense putting up neon markers to indicate that I was up to something special﻿—but I had a better one than that. I had to give Pat a chance to get in touch with me.

It didn’t work out that way.

I began walking down toward the Transolar revetment, using a shortcut street, looking around for Pat. It was a cinch she’d had some kind of a tail on me, and I was expecting to see her step out of almost any of the doorways I passed.

Instead, I heard something.

===Chinese===
一下午我都用在听取任务简报上。有一件事总算放下了心——既然我有官方指令接这桩活儿，S.B.I. 就会盯着我的一举一动。这可大不一样：无论我陷进什么样的乱子，至少会有人知道我出了什么事，而最要紧的是，知道为什么。

他们发了我一套公司飞行服，还有斯特米的胯挂枪套。我穿戴齐整，被一辆拉着遮光帘的汽车送到离港区一个街区的地方。

不到太空港门口下车，是我的主意。给魏德曼的理由也足够充分——犯不上挂起霓虹灯招牌，昭告人人我在干一桩特殊的勾当——但我还有个更好的理由。我得给帕特一个跟我联系上的机会。

事情没有照那样发展。

我抄近道街，朝特兰索拉的停机围场走去，一路留神找帕特。她肯定派了什么人盯我，我料着她会从我经过的随便哪个门洞里闪出来。

结果，我听见了动静。

===Original===
Back up the street, the way I had come, boot soles whispered on concrete. I turned around and looked, buried in shadow.

I couldn’t see anything. I turned back around, and kept on walking, and I heard a holster being unsnapped. I stopped to listen, and there was only silence. I moved, and somebody slipped a safety catch.

I leaped suddenly to my right. My shoulders touched the wall of a house. My hands blurred forward, one locking on my holster and holding it down, the other scooping the Sturmey out and clear of the leather, then blurring again as I shot my hand as far away from me as I could, fired down the street, and spun myself away from the building. I fired again, and the street lamp above my head smashed into bits. Then I was in a deep doorway, crouched, waiting, while ribbons of light cut creases in the wall where I’d been.

That was how it began. There were endless minutes of silence, and then someone would drag a heel or kick a step. There’d be the kick of my gun against my palm, and once, the count on their side dropped from five to four.

A dot of light flickered from behind a high gutter, and rock chipped off a wall near my head. I ducked, kissed the sidewalk with my belly, slithered down a flight of steps to a basement alcove, rolled over, and slid behind the stone. On the way down, I fired back, and I heard a rasp of metal on stone. Not the momentary rake of a belt buckle or button, but a gun, dragging its muzzle against curbing while the man who’d fired it kicked his life away in the gutter. I heard it drop the last inch to the street.

===Chinese===
身后街上，我来时的方向，靴底在混凝土上窸窣作响。我回身望去，那里漆黑一片。

什么也看不见。我转回头继续走，听见枪套扣被解开的声音。我停下细听，只有寂静。我一迈步，就有人拨开了保险。

我猛地向右一纵。双肩抵上一栋楼的外墙。双手闪电般抢出，一只死死按住自己的枪套不让它翘起，另一只把斯特米从皮套里捞出来、脱离枪套，随即又是一阵残影——我把持枪的手臂尽量朝远处探，朝街那头开了一枪，人同时旋身离开墙体。再补一枪，头顶的路灯炸成碎片。然后我已蹲进一处深凹的门洞，等着，一道道光带把我刚才立足的墙面割出道道沟痕。

事情就是这样开的头。是无边无际的死寂，偶尔有人拖一下脚跟，或踢一下台阶。我的枪一次次磕在掌心上，而有一次，他们那头的人数从五个变成了四个。

一点光从高处排水槽后面闪了一下，我脑袋旁的墙上崩掉一块石屑。我伏低，肚皮贴着人行道，顺一段台阶溜到地下室的壁龛前，一个翻滚，滑进石壁后面。下滑途中我回了一枪，听见金属刮擦石头的声音。不是皮带扣或纽扣擦过的一瞬间，而是一支枪，枪口蹭着路缘石拖行——开枪那人正在排水沟里蹬掉他最后一点命。我听见那支枪落下最后一寸，磕在街面上。

---

===Original===
I knew they’d be flanking me pretty soon. I heard cloth whisper as two of them slipped off to each side. The fellow they’d left behind began firing from all angles, weaving back and forth to cover them. He put too much pattern in his weave, though, and that was his mistake. The pattern broke, and became random as the guns spun out of his hands before he could even realize there was a shot coming.

Two! I rolled away from behind the steps, crouched, and padded away on the balls of my feet. My boots had special sponge soles on them, but even so, a lance of blue slashed from down the street against my calf. I plowed into the sidewalk, furrowing my face and tearing meat off the knuckles wrapped around my gun. I tried not to catch my breath too loudly as I dragged myself behind the ornamental outcrop of the bannister on the next flight of steps.

My leg felt like there was a railroad spike driven into it, and my knuckles were numb and stiff. I worked my fingers to keep them from freezing up on me, even though jolts of pain came up and hammered at the backs of my eyes. My face felt wet and itchy. I lay there, waiting.

I got one more of them. He decided I was dead, and poked his pale face out against a black wall. The face vanished in a burst of red, and he sprawled back. I chuckled.

There wasn’t much I could do but chuckle. The one guy left had me cold. I had no idea where he was, but he’d seen the flash of my gun. I couldn’t shift position fast enough or quietly enough to get away. All I could do was lie there.

===Chinese===
我知道他们很快就会包抄我。两个人分头向两侧溜开时，我听见衣料的窸窣声。留下断后的那个开始从各个角度开火，来回穿插着掩护同伙。可惜他的穿插路数太有规律，那便是他的死因。规律一乱，火力散了架，双枪脱手飞出，他甚至没意识到有一枪正在飞来。

两个了！我从台阶后面滚开，猫低身子，踮着脚尖疾行。靴子装着特制海绵底，可即便如此，一道蓝色光矛还是从街那头劈中我的小腿。我脸朝下犁进人行道，脸皮擦出沟，攥枪的指节撕掉了肉。我把自己拖到下一段台阶栏杆的装饰凸石后面，尽量不让自己喘息出声。

腿里像钉进了一根铁路道钉，指节又麻又僵。我活动手指，不让它们彻底罢工，尽管一阵阵痛楚直冲上来，锤打着我的眼后。脸上又湿又痒。我趴在那里，等着。

我又收拾了他们一个。那家伙认定我死了，把一张苍白的脸探出来，衬在黑墙上。那张脸在一团红光中消失，他仰面摊开。我低低笑了。

除了低笑我也做不了什么。剩下那最后一个家伙把我吃得死死的。我不知道他在哪儿，他却看见了我的枪口火光。我转移位置，速度不够快，动静也压不住，跑不掉。我只能趴着不动。

===Original===
He took a chance and jumped me. I never heard him coming.

A gun bounced off my head, and I went under﻿—But not before I looked up and saw that it was Pat herself.

===Chinese===
他孤注一掷，朝我扑了过来。我来不及听见他的动静。

一支枪磕在我头上，我便沉了下去——但在沉下去之前，我抬头看见了：那是帕特本人。

### III

===Original===
I remember lying on my back for quite awhile before I wanted to open my eyes. I knew I wasn’t on the street. The air was warm, and heated, and I was on a bed, or something like it. My leg was giving me hell where it had been burned, but I could feel the pressure of a bandage. I couldn’t tell about my hand and face﻿—they felt as if something had been done about them, too, but I couldn’t find out for sure without looking or touching them, and I didn’t want to do that yet.

*Why the hell had Pat jumped me?* I couldn’t figure it.

I opened my eyes, and she was standing over me, a gun dangling from one hand. I threw a look at my watch, and saw I’d been out a half hour, at most.

“What the hell﻿—” I began.

She cut me off with a gesture of the gun. “Shut up,” she said wearily. “You’ll have plenty of time to start lying later.” She grimaced with tired disgust.

I shook my head, but I knew better than to go on talking. There was anger working its way into the hurt look in her eyes.

===Chinese===
我记得自己仰面躺了好一阵子，才愿意睁眼。我知道自己不在街上了。空气温热，是暖气烘出来的，我躺在一张床上，或是什么类似床的东西上。小腿烧伤的地方疼得要命，但能感觉到绷带勒着的压力。手和脸的情况说不准——感觉也像是被处理过了，可不看不用手摸就没法确认，而我暂时还不想那么做。

*帕特他妈的为什么要偷袭我？*我想不明白。

我睁开眼，她正俯身站在我上方，一只手垂着枪。我瞥了眼手表，看见我至多昏了半个钟头。

「搞什么名堂——」我开了腔。

她用枪一摆，止住我。「闭嘴。」她倦倦地说，「往后有的是时间让你开始撒谎。」她疲惫而厌恶地皱起脸。

我摇了摇头，但知道不能再往下说。她眼里受伤的神情，正被怒气一点点浸透。

===Original===
I got up, ignoring the feeling in my calf, and noticed several other things. I’d been lying on a low couch. My flying boots were unzipped, so that I couldn’t move faster than a shuffle. The coveralls were loose around my waist where my harness had been.

I pressed my left upper arm against my ribs. As far as I could tell, they hadn’t found my insurance policy﻿—a little singleshot burner hidden between two of my ribs under a strip of what looked like skin. There was collodion on my face, and tape on my knuckles.

“Happy?” she asked.

“Uh-huh. I’m Prince Charming, you’re Snow White, and, as far as I can add up, somebody’s fresh out of dwarves. What’s going on around here, anyway?”

“You double-crossed me, that’s what happened. We made a deal, and you sold out on it!” She was working herself to boiling mad, clear through﻿—and that explained why she’d looked at me the way she had.

I shook my head again, trying to clear it. I was getting mad myself.

===Chinese===
我起身，顾不上小腿里的感觉，同时注意到另外几件事。我一直躺在一张矮榻上。飞行靴的拉链被拉开了，这样我就快不过拖着脚挪步。连体服在腰部装具原先系着的地方松松垮垮。

我把左上臂压向肋部。就我判断，他们还没发现我的保命符——一支藏在两根肋骨之间、覆着一条看似皮肤的东西下面的单发射线枪。我脸上敷了火棉胶，指节缠了胶布。

「满意了？」她问。

「嗯哼。我是白马王子，你是白雪公主，照我算下来，就是有小矮人，也刚被人用光了。这儿到底在演哪一出？」

「你出卖了我，就这么回事。我们说好了合作，你却把它卖了！」她的火气正从里到外把自己烧到沸腾——这也解释了她方才为什么那样看我。

我又摇了摇头，想让自己清醒。我自己也开始上火了。

===Original===
“Look, Pat, I can take just so much mysterious crap, and no more,” I said, feeling the blood starting to work itself into my face. “I got in from Venus, after winding up one of the prettiest insurrections you ever saw. I got my belly full of the sound of guns and the smell of death, and all I wanted to do was relax and spend the dough I made. No sooner do I take my first drink of decent liquor in six months than you walk up to me and start the goddamdest mess I’ve ever been in!

“All right﻿—we made a deal. As far as I know, I’ve carried out the orders you gave me. I got the job for Transolar, and I started it. Nobody but you and I know there’s something funny going on, though I suppose the cops are starting to suspect﻿—seeing as I’ve killed five men in two days, and helped you knock off two more. Now let’s get a few things straight around here! I’ve been shot at, slugged, and generally treated like a supporting star in a cloak and dagger movie. Either I get some fast answers, or I start slugging!”

===Chinese===
「听着，帕特，这种神神秘秘的屁话，我能忍的有限。」我说，感觉血正在往脸上涌。「我刚从金星回来，收了你生平所见最漂亮的一场叛乱的尾。枪声和死亡的气味我灌了一肚子，只想歇歇，把挣来的钱花一花。六个月来头一口像样的酒刚下肚，你就找上门来，把我拖进我这辈子碰上的最他妈糟糕的一摊烂事！

「好——我们说定了合作。据我所知，你给我的指令我都照办了。特兰索拉的活儿我接了，也开了工。除了你我，没人知道这里面有蹊跷，不过我看警方也快起疑了——毕竟我两天里杀了五个人，还帮你干掉了两个。现在，把这儿的事情给我说清楚几件！我挨枪打、挨棍敲，活像间谍片里一个跑龙套的。要么痛痛快快给我答案，要么我动手开揍！」

===Original===
I’d been moving forward as I talked, getting madder and madder, and closer to being ready to dive for that gun and rip it out of her hand.

She was starting to lose some of her determination. The gun muzzle was dipping. I reached out my hand.

The gun was centered on me again in an instant, but the fire was gone out of her eyes.

“Hold it, Ash!” she said. “You sound too mad to be lying, but you haven’t convinced me yet. Just stay put a minute. You want to know what’s going on? You should have a pretty fair idea by now,” she went on, still keeping the gun on me. “I’m after that power pile you’re supposed to fly out to Titan. Harry needs it.”

===Chinese===
说话间我一直朝她逼近，火气越蹿越高，也越来越接近扑上去夺枪的临界点。

她的决心开始瓦解。枪口渐渐垂了下去。我伸出手。

枪口转瞬间又对准我，但她眼里的火已经熄了。

「别动，阿什！」她说。「你火气这么大，听起来不像撒谎，但你还没说服我。先在原地待一会儿。你想知道出了什么事？到现在你该有个八九不离十的判断了。」她继续说，枪仍对着我。「我盯的就是你那趟要送去泰坦的动力堆燃料。哈里需要它。」

---

===Original===
I should have known, I suppose. Well, maybe she was still space-struck. Thorsten played rough, and he had some strange friends, but so far he hadn’t earned a full-scale visit from the T.S.N. It didn’t mean as much in this case, though. He would have been a tough nut to crack, sitting out there in the Asteroids with a good-sized fleet behind him. Still﻿—

But that was for another time. I let her see by my face that the subject wasn’t closed, and then I went on.

“Yeah﻿—keep talking. Who jumped you on Rocket Row last night? Why were you trying to pot me a while ago?”

“Because﻿—goddam it, I don’t know *what* to think!” she said. “Those were S.B.I. men last night. I knew they were trailing me, but I thought I’d gotten rid of them before I contacted you. Maybe I did﻿—maybe they picked me up again when I went back out on the street. Anyway, we killed them, but the S.B.I. knows damn well who did it. We did enough yelling back and forth to let all of New York City know who it was.”

That had been a dumb play, all right. I didn’t have time to curse my stupidity, though. I didn’t care one bit for the idea of me having shot an S.B.I. man. It was his own fault, but it wouldn’t help my record any.

“All right,” I said, “so they were S.B.I. men. That’s tough﻿—for them.”

===Chinese===
我早该想到的，大概。唉，也许她还是当年的太空迷。索斯滕手段狠，交的朋友也三教九流，但迄今为止，他还没混到让 T.S.N. 大举兴师问罪的地步。不过这一次，这话的分量也没多重。他坐在小行星带里，身后有一支颇具规模的舰队，本就是块难啃的硬骨头。可话又说回来——

但那是另说的事。我让她从我脸上看出这个话题没完，然后接着问。

「说下去。昨晚在火箭街偷袭你的是谁？刚才你又为什么想干掉我？」

「因为——真见鬼，我都不知道*该*怎么想了！」她说。「昨晚那些是 S.B.I. 的人。我知道他们在盯我的梢，但联系你之前，我以为已经甩掉他们了。也许真甩掉了——也许是我重新上街时又被他们缀上了。总之，我们把人杀了，而 S.B.I. 对是谁干的清楚得很。咱们俩那通你来我往的喊杀，足够让整个纽约城都知道是谁干的了。」

那步棋走得确实蠢。可我没工夫骂自己蠢。一想到我朝 S.B.I. 的人开过枪，我就浑身不自在。那是他自己找的，但对我的档案没半点好处。

「行，」我说，「就算他们是 S.B.I. 的人。那可不妙——对他们而言。」

===Original===
“Why haven’t we been picked up? I’ve been hiding out all day﻿—but how did you get away with walking in Transolar in broad daylight and coming out again, if you didn’t make some kind of deal?” She was gnawing on her lip. “Damn it, give me a reasonable explanation, and I’ll forget the whole thing.”

That sent me off. I knew why I hadn’t been picked up, all right﻿—they were waiting for me to blow this deal open for them. Maybe, if I did that, they’d forget I’d killed one of them. I’d have to do a really good job, though.

But I wasn’t doing too much reasoning, right then. I’d been mad all night, but that was nothing to what I felt right then.

I could feel a big red ball of pure rage building up inside me. My fingers started to tremble, and my vision got hazy.

I swung out my hand and slapped the muzzle of the gun as hard as I could, and to hell with what it did to my bum hand. The gun went spinning away, taking skin off her fingers as it went, and crashed into a wall. I swung my hand back and slapped her across the face. She fell back and hit the floor. She lay huddled in a corner, looking up at me, her eyes wide and her mouth open with surprise.

“You’ll forget the whole thing, huh? All I have to do is explain away some half-baked idea that came into your head, and you’ll forgive me, is that it?” I reached down, grabbed her shoulder, pulled her to her feet, and held her there. Her mouth was still open, and she couldn’t get any words out of her throat.

===Chinese===
「那为什么我们还没被抓起来？我躲了一整天——可你要是没跟他们做什么交易，怎么敢大白天走进特兰索拉再走出来？」她咬着嘴唇。「见鬼，给我一个说得通的解释，我就把这事整个揭过去。」

这话把我点着了。我没被抓的原因，我心里雪亮——他们在等着我替他们把这笔买卖的底掀开。也许我做到了，他们就会忘掉我杀过他们一个人。不过，那可得干得真漂亮才行。

可那一刻我已顾不上多想。我整晚都在冒火，但跟我此刻的心头火比，那根本不算什么。

我能感觉到一团纯粹的怒火，红彤彤地，正在我胸中越鼓越大。手指开始发颤，眼前的景象发起糊来。

我抡出手掌，用尽全力拍在枪口上，那只伤手怎么样见鬼去吧。枪旋转着飞出去，带下了她手指上的皮，砸在墙上。我手一挥回，一巴掌抽在她脸上。她仰面摔倒，跌在地上，缩在墙角，仰头望着我，眼睛瞪得溜圆，嘴惊得合不拢。

「你就把整件事揭过去，是吧？我只需把你脑子里冒出来的哪个半生不熟的念头解释清楚，你就会原谅我，是不是这个意思？」我俯身抓住她的肩膀，把她拽起来，按在那儿。她的嘴还张着，喉咙里却挤不出一个字。

===Original===
“You’re going to *forgive* me for getting me into a deal that involves killing S.B.I. men. You’re going to forgive me for having a guy that used to be a buddy of mine hate my guts, I suppose. You’re going to forgive me for slapping my face, and I’m going to get your gracious pardon for having to fight it out for my life tonight against five guns. That’s just fine! Is that supposed to cover getting shot and knocked around and slugged?”

I hauled back and slapped her again. “And that’s for pointing a gun at me! Twice. I live by a gun, and I expect to die by one, someday. But not at the hands of a woman who can’t fight a man on his own terms, and has to keep him off with a gun after she gets herself into a mess. All right﻿—you know how to use one. But, so help me, you wave one of those things at me again, and I’ll ram it down your throat catty-cornered!”

I pushed her away, and she slammed back against the wall. “One more thing,” I said. “Have you ever heard of the S.B.I. fooling around making deals with a guy that’s killed one of their men? Not on your life! They’re a tough crew, and a smart one. If they thought I had anything to do with that fracas last night, I’d be on my way to a Federal gas chamber right now, if I was lucky enough to live through the working-over they’d give me! Use your brains!”

===Chinese===
「你要*原谅*我，为的是把我拖进一桩牵扯到枪杀 S.B.I. 人员的买卖。我猜，你还要原谅我，因为我害一个从前和我称兄道弟的家伙恨我入骨。你要原谅我打了我自己的耳光，而我呢，还要领你的恩赦，为的是今晚不得不在五支枪下为保命死拼。好得很！挨枪、挨砸、挨闷棍，这些就算都抵过了？」

我抡圆了又是一巴掌。「这是为你拿枪指我！两次。我靠枪吃饭，也料想早晚有一天死在枪下。但绝不是死在一个不敢跟男人当面锣对面鼓、把自己陷进烂事之后只会拿枪拦人的女人手里。好吧——你确实会用枪。可我发誓，你再敢拿那种玩意儿冲我晃，我就把它斜着塞进你的喉咙！」

我把她推开，她撞回墙上。「还有一件事。」我说。「你听说过 S.B.I. 跟一个杀了他们自己人的家伙做什么交易吗？做梦去吧！他们是一帮硬角色，也是一帮精明人。他们要是认为我昨晚那场混战里有份，这会儿我早就在押往联邦毒气室的路上了——还得托他们的拷问手下留情，我命大活得下来！动动脑子！」

===Original===
She stood against the wall, staring at me, making sounds in her throat. One of her cheeks was starting to puff.

I started for her again. Her eyes got even wider.

“Ash!”

Her voice was high and frightened. Somehow, it cut through the deadly anger in my chest, and made me stop.

“Ash! Please﻿—Ash﻿—I.﻿ ﻿…” She put her hands up to her face and stood there, sobbing into them.

===Chinese===
她抵着墙站着，直勾勾瞪着我，喉咙里发出细碎的声音。她的一边脸颊开始肿起来。

我又朝她逼过去。她的眼睛瞪得更大了。

「阿什！」

她的嗓音尖而发颤。不知怎么，它穿透了我胸口那团要命的怒火，让我停了下来。

「阿什！求你——阿什——我……」她双手捂住脸，站在那里，对着掌心啜泣。

===Original===
My nails were digging into my palms. I opened my hands, and saw blood running over my knuckles where the tape had torn away. There was some of my blood on her dress, where I’d grabbed her shoulder.

“Ash! Please﻿—I’m sorry﻿—It﻿—it’s just that I didn’t know what to think.”

I don’t know how I got over to her, but then I had my arms around her, and she was digging her teeth into the cloth of my shoulder, and sobbing.

“Pat, why do you have to be this way? Why can’t you﻿—” I was saying, and stroking that red-brown hair. She wasn’t a tough, self-assured woman who could gun a man down without blinking. She was a soft, hurt, crying girl, mumbling through tears, her body shaking.

I wasn’t a guy who’d fought his way through a war and countless battles since, either.

===Chinese===
我的指甲正掐进掌心。我摊开手，看见血漫过指节，胶布那里已经撕裂。她的裙子上也沾了我的血，在我抓过她肩膀的地方。

「阿什！求你——对不起——我——我只是不知道该怎么想了。」

我不知道自己是怎么走到她跟前的，反正接着我已把她搂在怀里，她的牙齿咬进我肩头的布料，呜咽着。

「帕特，你何苦这样？你就不能——」我一边说，一边抚着那头红棕色的头发。她不是那种眼都不眨就能放倒一个男人的剽悍自负的女人。她是个柔软的、受了伤的、哭泣的姑娘，含着泪喃喃不清，身子发着抖。

我也不是什么从一场战争和此后无数场厮杀里杀出来的硬汉了。

===Original===
She pulled her face away from me, and looked up. Her eyes were wet, but she wasn’t scared any more.

I looked down at her. I started to say something, but she stopped me.

“I had it coming, Ash,” she said softly. “I didn’t trust you. I should have known better.”

She half-smiled. “I haven’t met too many people who could get worked up over not being trusted.”

I couldn’t look at her. I was going to have to turn her over to the S.B.I. some day, and I couldn’t look at her.

===Chinese===
她把脸从我肩头抬起，仰起头来。她的眼睛湿着，但不再害怕了。

我低头看她。我正要说什么，她止住了我。

「是我自找的，阿什。」她轻声说。「我不信任你。我本该更明白些。」

她半嗔半笑。「会为不被信任而动这么大气的男人，我见过的可不多。」

我不敢看她。早晚有一天，我得把她交给 S.B.I.，我不敢看她。

===Original===
“Ash, remember the night you spanked me? Remember what you did first?”

I felt her hand on my face, turning it. Then she was kissing me, her lips soft and fresh, her wet face under my glance, her long lashes down over closed eyes. Her arms moved on my back, and her body was as light as a dream in my arms.

My own eyes closed.

===Chinese===
「阿什，还记得你打我屁股的那晚吗？记得你先做了什么？」

我感觉到她的手贴上我的脸，把它扳过来。然后她吻住了我，双唇柔软而清新，泪湿的脸在我目光下，长长的睫毛垂覆在闭着的眼睛上。她的手臂在我背上移动，她的身子在我怀里轻得像一个梦。

我闭上了自己的眼睛。
### IV

===Original===
Flight coveralls are designed to be airtight when fully zipped. Hoods with transparent faceplates and oxygen leads can be hermetically sealed to the collars, and every ship has emergency plug-ins for the oxygen tubes. In combat, all spacemen keep their hoods thrown back, like mackinaw hoods, so that if a hole is blown in the hull, they can slip the hoods on and plug into the emergency oxygen supply. Struggling into a full-dress spacesuit is too complicated a job to entrust to the few frantic minutes that spell the difference between life and death, and meanwhile, the coveralls are far more comfortable in flight.

Besides, anyone who’d seen what a spacesuit does to a figure like Pat’s will agree that it’s a dirty shame.

While Pat was climbing into her outfit, I was outlining the plan we’d have to follow. As long as I was going to go along with this offer of hers, temporarily, at least, I might as well do it right.

===Chinese===
飞行连体服的设计是拉链拉满即气密。带透明面罩和氧气管接口的头盔可以密封对接在领口上，每艘船都有供氧气管插接的应急接口。作战时，所有宇航员都把头盔向后翻开，像粗呢大衣的兜帽一样挂着，这样一旦船壳被打破一个洞，就能立即罩上头盔，接进应急供氧。挣扎着穿全套宇航服这种活计太复杂，赌不起那生死一线的几分钟；何况连体服在飞行中要舒服得多。

再说，凡是见过宇航服对帕特那种身材干出什么好事的人，都会承认那简直是暴殄天物。

帕特往她那身行头里钻的时候，我把必须执行的方案给她讲了个大概。反正我打算顺着她这个提议走——至少暂时是——那不如就办得像样些。

===Original===
“I got into a cab accident, or something,” I said. “That accounts for the shape I’m in. You’re an old friend of mine, and since I’m in no condition to fly and fight at the same time, I’m taking you along as copilot.

“Weidmann’ll stick me for your pay, of course. I’ll make sure he does﻿—that way there won’t be much kick about you coming along, especially if I make it a ‘both or neither’ proposition.

“When we get out in space, you show me how to get to Thorsten’s bubble in the Asteroids, and that’s it. We deliver the pile charge, shoot back out into space, fake the signs of a big battle, and yell for help over the radio. There’ll be a squawk about you being a woman then, of course, but hell, us spacebums are supposed to be devil-may-care, aren’t we?”

===Chinese===
「我出了趟出租车事故，还是什么的。」我说，「这就解释了我这副德行。你是我的一位老朋友，我又没本事一边开船一边开枪，所以带你同去，当副驾驶。

「魏德曼自然会从我这儿抠一份你的工钱。我还会保证他抠——这样你同行就不会有什么异议，尤其我要是把话说成『要么俩人都去，要么都不去』。

「等我们进了太空，你指给我怎么去小行星带里索斯滕的气泡舱，就这样。我们卸下堆芯燃料，反身杀回太空，伪造一场大战的痕迹，再用无线电呼救。到那时你是个女人的事当然会招来一片哗然，可去他的，我们这些太空流浪汉本就该天不怕地不怕，不是吗？」

===Original===
It was a great little plan, all right. It would give S.B.I. the location of Thorsten’s base, and it wouldn’t hold up delivery of the pile charge any longer than it would take to salvage it. Meanwhile, space would be rid of Harry.

“Sounds like it’ll work, all right,” she said. “I wish I was surer the S.B.I. didn’t have anything big on me. It’ll be a bad enough stink as it is.” She grinned. “But we’ll make out.”

===Chinese===
这小计划确实妙。它能把索斯滕基地的位置交给 S.B.I.，堆芯燃料的交付也至多耽搁一个打捞回收的工夫。与此同时，太空里就此少了个哈里。

「听着是行得通。」她说，「只是我希望更有把握 S.B.I. 手里没捏着我的大案底。照现在这样，已经够臭一壶的了。」她咧嘴一笑。「但我们应付得过去。」

---

===Original===
Weidmann was out at the field, fuming over the fact that I was an hour and a half late.

He surprised me, though. He didn’t boggle over taking Pat along, once I gave him a story about being lightly hit by a car and having to take my friend along.

Pat had had a tight cloth strapped across her breasts, her hood over her face, and I’d gotten her into the ship fast.

“Okay, okay, who gives a damn what happens to you, as long as the job’s done,” Weidmann said, but I couldn’t believe him, somehow, when he added, “I don’t even care who does it, personally.”

He slipped an envelope into my pocket. “Something for you,” he said. “Don’t open it until you’re past Mars, and don’t let your friend see it﻿—for awhile, anyway.” He chuckled, and surprised me by doing it. He looked secretly happy over something, as if he knew about something awful that was going to happen to me. “You’ll have some sweet explaining to do to your friend, Holcomb. I’d love to see it.” But there was still that note of something more than laughter, more than most feelings, in his voice.

===Chinese===
魏德曼在机场等着，为我迟到了一个半钟头直冒火。

不过他倒让我吃了一惊。我一说是被汽车轻轻撞了一下、不得不带上我这位朋友，他对帕特同行竟没大惊小怪。

帕特胸前勒着束胸布，头盔罩着脸，我很快把她弄上了船。

「行了，行了，只要活儿办成，谁管你们死活。」魏德曼说。可不知怎么，他接下来那句我没法信：「说真的，这事由谁来做，我压根儿都不在乎。」

他把一个信封塞进我口袋。「给你的。」他说，「过了火星再拆，也别让你那位朋友看见——至少先别让她看见。」他轻声笑了，笑本身倒让我意外。他看上去为着什么事暗自高兴，仿佛知道有什么可怕的事要落到我头上。「到时候你可有得跟你那位朋友解释了，霍尔科姆。真想亲眼瞧瞧。」可他话音里仍带着那种超出笑意、超出寻常情绪的东西。

===Original===
He wouldn’t say more than that. He just shoved me into the ship and slammed the hatch.

I kept watching him in the starboard screens as we checked off the instrument board. He was a little figure at the edge of the field, staring wistfully up at the ship, his mechanical arm in his pocket.
